11. Core Schema and Core Types

All types are normative API surface for ibg-core unless noted otherwise. Lightweight signatures only — no method bodies.

11.1 Primitive Identifiers

Type Definition Semantics
NodeId struct NodeId(u32) Opaque, dense, 0-indexed within a BuildGraph instance; NOT stable across graph reconstructions unless mapped via NodeKey
ActionId struct ActionId(u32) Dense index into the action table; scoped to a BuildGraph
Revision struct Revision(u64) Monotone global version counter; 0 = "never evaluated"; bumped atomically before each build wave
Digest struct Digest([u8; 32]) blake3 output; content-addressed identity; fixed-size, Copy, Ord, Hash
NodeKey struct NodeKey(Arc<str>) Stable cross-invocation identity string (e.g., canonical path or logical name); maps to NodeId via graph index
  • NodeId, ActionId: Copy, PartialEq, Eq, Hash, Ord; serialized as u32 (little-endian).
  • Revision: Copy, PartialEq, Eq, Ord; serialized as u64 LE; monotonicity enforced by AtomicU64 in ibg-engine.
  • Digest: Copy, PartialEq, Eq, Hash, Ord; Display as lowercase hex-64; no constructors outside ibg-fingerprint.
  • NodeKey: Clone, PartialEq, Eq, Hash; interned at graph-construction time; persisted as UTF-8 string.

11.2 ActionKey

ActionKey(Digest)
  = blake3( command_bytes || NUL
         || concat_sorted(input_path → input_digest: path_bytes || NUL || digest_bytes)
         || concat_sorted(env k → v: k_bytes || NUL || v_bytes || NUL)
         || tool_digest_bytes )
  • struct ActionKey(Digest) — newtype; Copy, Eq, Hash.
  • Determinism invariant: same command + same sorted input digests + same env + same tool digest → identical ActionKey everywhere.
  • Undeclared input violates this: an input not in Action.inputs can change without changing ActionKey → cache returns stale result → correctness bug → sandbox must catch first.
  • ActionKey is the primary key in ibg-cache action-cache table.
  • Serialized as 32 raw bytes; human-readable as hex-64.

11.3 NodeKind and BuildNode

enum NodeKind {
    Source { path: PathBuf },                          // leaf: file on disk; no deps except via Glob
    Action(ActionId),                                  // internal: drives execution
    Glob   { pattern: String, base: PathBuf },         // expands to set of Source nodes
    Group,                                             // virtual aggregator; no action; pure fan-in
}

struct BuildNode {
    id:               NodeId,
    key:              NodeKey,
    kind:             NodeKind,
    deps:             Vec<NodeId>,          // static declared dependencies (edges)
    declared_outputs: Vec<PathBuf>,         // output paths this node is responsible for
}
  • BuildNode is immutable after BuildGraph::freeze().
  • deps encodes ONLY static edges; dynamic edges live in DynamicDep table (§11.5).
  • Ownership: BuildGraph owns all BuildNodes; external code holds NodeId handles.
  • Serialized via serde (bincode for persist, JSON for debug/CLI).
  • Compatibility: NodeId values may change across serialized graph versions; only NodeKey is stable.

11.4 Edge and Graph Topology

enum EdgeKind { Static, Dynamic }

struct Edge {
    from: NodeId,
    to:   NodeId,
    kind: EdgeKind,
}
  • Static edges declared at graph-construction time; part of BuildNode.deps.
  • Dynamic edges discovered during execution of an Action; recorded in DynamicDep table; must not introduce cycles (engine checks at discovery time).
  • Edge set is append-only during a build session (dynamic adds only); no edge deletion mid-session.
  • Persistence: full edge list flushed to ibg-persist at session end; loaded at next invocation.

11.5 DynamicDep

struct DynamicDep {
    from:            NodeId,
    to:              NodeId,
    discovered_at:   Revision,
}
  • Stored in a separate table from static edges; reverse-dep index updated atomically on discovery.
  • Cycle check: before recording, run DFS from to to confirm from not reachable; on cycle → IbgError::CycleDetected.
  • Persistence semantics: durable per session; stale dynamic deps from prior sessions are retained until the discovering action re-runs and re-declares (or drops) them.

11.6 Action

struct Cmd {
    program:  PathBuf,
    args:     Vec<OsString>,
}

type EnvMap = BTreeMap<String, String>;   // sorted for deterministic ActionKey hashing

struct Action {
    id:      ActionId,
    command: Cmd,
    inputs:  Vec<NodeId>,                 // must be complete; undeclared = sandbox violation
    outputs: Vec<PathBuf>,                // declared outputs; capture enforced post-exec
    env:     EnvMap,
    key:     ActionKey,                   // computed eagerly at graph-construction time
}
  • Immutable after construction; key must equal ActionKey::compute(&self) — enforced by constructor.
  • inputs references NodeIds whose output_digest must be resolved before ActionKey can be finalized (fingerprint phase).
  • Mutation semantics: none; if inputs change, a new ActionKey is computed by ibg-fingerprint.
  • Concurrency: shared via Arc<Action> across scheduler threads; no interior mutation.
  • Serialized: full Action (minus key, recomputed on load) to ibg-persist.

11.7 NodeState and NodeStatus

enum NodeStatus { Clean, Dirty, Running, Failed }

struct NodeState {
    last_verified: Revision,           // Revision at which node was confirmed up-to-date
    last_changed:  Revision,           // Revision at which output_digest last differed
    output_digest: Option<Digest>,     // blake3 of declared outputs bundle; None if never built
    status:        NodeStatus,
}
  • Invariants:
    • last_verified ≤ current_global_revision always.
    • last_changed ≤ last_verified always (changed implies previously verified).
    • status == Clean iff last_verified == current_global_revision.
    • output_digest.is_some() iff node has been successfully built at least once.
  • Mutation: only ibg-engine eval loop mutates NodeState; guarded by per-node RwLock or epoch-based concurrency in MemoTable.
  • Persistence: full NodeState written to ibg-persist on flush; loaded at startup.
  • Early-cutoff predicate: (new_output_digest == state.output_digest) → set last_verified = current_revision; do NOT update last_changed; dependents remain Clean.

11.8 VerifiedSet

struct VerifiedSet {
    inner: DashMap<NodeId, NodeState>,
}
  • DashMap for concurrent reads during parallel eval; sharded by NodeId bucket.
  • ibg-engine owns one VerifiedSet per build session.
  • After BuildGraph::freeze(), set is populated lazily as nodes are evaluated.
  • NOT persisted directly; NodeState entries are extracted and flushed to ibg-persist.

11.9 MemoTable

struct MemoTable {
    states:   VerifiedSet,
    in_flight: DashMap<NodeId, Arc<tokio::sync::watch::Receiver<NodeStatus>>>,
}
  • in_flight prevents duplicate concurrent evaluation of the same node (demand-driven dedup).
  • A node evaluation that finds an in_flight entry awaits its watch::Receiver instead of spawning a duplicate task.
  • Invariant: a NodeId is either in states (evaluated), in in_flight (running), or neither (not yet demanded).
  • Concurrency: all operations lock-free on read path; DashMap shard lock on write.

11.10 BuildGraph

struct BuildGraph {
    nodes:    Vec<BuildNode>,                          // dense NodeId → BuildNode
    actions:  Vec<Action>,                             // dense ActionId → Action
    edges:    Vec<Edge>,                               // static + dynamic (append-only post-freeze)
    node_idx: HashMap<NodeKey, NodeId>,                // stable-key lookup
    revdep:   Vec<Vec<NodeId>>,                        // reverse-dep index: NodeId → dependents
    topo:     Vec<NodeId>,                             // topological order (Kahn's, recomputed on dynamic-dep add)
    frozen:   bool,
}

impl BuildGraph {
    fn add_node(&mut self, key: NodeKey, kind: NodeKind) -> NodeId;
    fn add_edge(&mut self, from: NodeId, to: NodeId) -> Result<(), GraphError>;
    fn freeze(&mut self) -> Result<(), GraphError>;    // topo sort + cycle check; sets frozen = true
    fn transitive_deps(&self, root: NodeId) -> impl Iterator<Item = NodeId>;
    fn transitive_rdeps(&self, root: NodeId) -> impl Iterator<Item = NodeId>;
    fn affected_set(&self, changed: &[NodeId]) -> BTreeSet<NodeId>;
}
  • add_node/add_edge panic if called after freeze().
  • freeze() is idempotent (second call is no-op after first succeeds).
  • revdep index updated on every add_edge; invalid after unfrozen dynamic-dep additions until topo is recomputed.
  • Ownership: Arc<BuildGraph> shared across engine + scheduler + watcher; mutations post-freeze only via interior dynamic-dep append (separate Mutex<Vec<Edge>> for dynamic edges).
  • Serialized: full graph to ibg-persist; node_idx reconstructed from nodes on load.

11.11 CacheEntry and CasObject

struct ExecMeta {
    duration_ms:  u64,
    exit_code:    i32,
    stdout_digest: Option<Digest>,
    stderr_digest: Option<Digest>,
    host:         String,
    timestamp_ms: u64,
}

struct CacheEntry {
    key:     ActionKey,
    outputs: Vec<(PathBuf, Digest)>,   // declared output path → CAS digest
    meta:    ExecMeta,
}

struct CasObject {
    digest: Digest,
    size:   u64,
    data:   Bytes,                     // lazily loaded; may be memory-mapped
}
  • CacheEntry: primary record in action-cache table; keyed by ActionKey (32 bytes).
  • CasObject: content of a single blob in CAS; identified solely by Digest.
  • Invariant: CasObject.digest == blake3(CasObject.data) always; checked on read; error on mismatch.
  • Persistence: CacheEntry → action-cache DB (redb); CasObject data → filesystem CAS store (flat file named by hex digest, or LMDB).
  • Concurrency: CAS reads are lock-free (immutable by content address); writes use file-system atomicity (write to .tmp, rename).
  • Compatibility: CacheEntry schema versioned; old entries with unknown fields are evicted, not migrated.

11.12 ActionCache Trait

trait ActionCache: Send + Sync {
    fn get(&self, key: &ActionKey) -> Result<Option<CacheEntry>, CacheError>;
    fn put(&self, entry: CacheEntry) -> Result<(), CacheError>;
    fn evict(&self, key: &ActionKey) -> Result<bool, CacheError>;
    fn gc(&self, live_keys: &BTreeSet<ActionKey>) -> Result<GcStats, CacheError>;
}
  • Local impl: redb-backed; async via spawn_blocking.
  • Remote impl: ibg-remote RemoteCacheClient; gRPC REAPI.
  • Both impls behind Arc<dyn ActionCache>; layered (local-then-remote) via TieredCache adapter.
  • gc() must be called at invocation end or via background GC task; never called concurrently with put.

11.13 CasStore Trait

trait CasStore: Send + Sync {
    fn get(&self, digest: &Digest) -> Result<Option<CasObject>, CacheError>;
    fn put(&self, data: Bytes) -> Result<Digest, CacheError>;      // returns computed digest
    fn contains(&self, digest: &Digest) -> Result<bool, CacheError>;
    fn delete(&self, digest: &Digest) -> Result<bool, CacheError>;
}
  • Invariant: put(data) is idempotent; same data → same Digest; second write is no-op.
  • get returns None iff no object with that digest; never returns corrupted data (checked on read).

11.14 BuildRequest and BuildResult

struct BuildRequest {
    targets:    Vec<NodeId>,
    max_jobs:   Option<usize>,          // overrides scheduler default
    force_rerun: bool,                  // bypass cache; always execute
    dry_run:    bool,                   // eval + cache-check only; no exec
}

struct BuildResult {
    built:    Vec<NodeId>,              // nodes that executed
    cached:   Vec<NodeId>,              // nodes served from cache
    skipped:  Vec<NodeId>,              // nodes that passed early-cutoff
    errors:   Vec<(NodeId, IbgError)>,
    stats:    BuildStats,
}

struct BuildStats {
    wall_ms:            u64,
    cache_hit_rate:     f32,            // cached / (built + cached); NaN if 0 actions
    critical_path_ms:   u64,
    total_actions:      u32,
    recompute_count:    u32,
}
  • BuildResult.errors is non-empty on partial failure; engine evaluates all requested targets before returning.
  • dry_run == true → built is empty; cached and skipped reflect what would have been done.
  • force_rerun does NOT invalidate the cache store; it bypasses lookup only; result is stored back.

11.15 IbgError

enum IbgError {
    Graph(GraphError),
    Fingerprint(FingerprintError),
    Cache(CacheError),
    Exec(ExecError),
    Sched(SchedError),
    Persist(PersistError),
    Sandbox(SandboxError),
    Remote(RemoteError),
    Io(std::io::Error),
}

enum GraphError {
    CycleDetected { path: Vec<NodeId> },
    NodeNotFound(NodeKey),
    DuplicateKey(NodeKey),
    GraphNotFrozen,
    GraphAlreadyFrozen,
    InvalidEdge { from: NodeId, to: NodeId, reason: &'static str },
}

enum CacheError {
    NotFound(ActionKey),
    Corrupted { digest: Digest, expected: Digest, actual: Digest },
    BackendError(String),
    SchemaMismatch { found: u32, expected: u32 },
}

enum ExecError {
    SpawnFailed { command: String, source: std::io::Error },
    NonZeroExit { code: i32, stderr_digest: Option<Digest> },
    OutputMissing { path: PathBuf },
    OutputDigestMismatch { path: PathBuf, expected: Digest, actual: Digest },
    Timeout { limit_ms: u64 },
}

enum SandboxError {
    UndeclaredInput { path: PathBuf, action: ActionId },
    UndeclaredOutput { path: PathBuf, action: ActionId },
    NonDeterministic { output: PathBuf, run1: Digest, run2: Digest },
    SandboxSetupFailed(String),
}

enum PersistError {
    CorruptState { detail: String },
    SchemaMismatch { found: u32, expected: u32 },
    WriteFailure(std::io::Error),
    MigrationFailed { from: u32, to: u32 },
}
  • All variants implement std::error::Error + Display.
  • IbgError is non_exhaustive; downstream crates must handle _ arm.
  • PersistError::CorruptState triggers full re-evaluation fallback (no incorrect incremental result).
  • SandboxError::UndeclaredInput always fails the build loudly; never silently ignored.

11.16 StatEntry and DigestCache

struct StatEntry {
    mtime_ns: i128,
    size:     u64,
    inode:    u64,
    dev:      u64,
}

struct DigestCache {
    inner: DashMap<PathBuf, (StatEntry, Digest)>,
    capacity: usize,
}

impl DigestCache {
    fn get_or_compute(&self, path: &Path) -> Result<Digest, FingerprintError>;
    fn invalidate(&self, path: &Path);
    fn invalidate_all(&self);
}
  • Fast-path: if (mtime_ns, size, inode, dev) matches cached entry, return cached Digest without re-hashing.
  • Invalidated by ibg-watch on fs events before the next eval wave.
  • Not persisted (rebuilt cheaply on next fingerprint request); avoids stale-stat bugs across daemon restarts.
  • Capacity eviction: LRU when len() > capacity; default capacity = 64 k entries.

11.17 GlobalRevision

struct GlobalRevision {
    inner: Arc<AtomicU64>,
}

impl GlobalRevision {
    fn current(&self) -> Revision;
    fn bump(&self) -> Revision;        // fetch_add(1, SeqCst); returns NEW value
    fn snapshot(&self) -> Revision;    // load(SeqCst)
}
  • One GlobalRevision per IncrementalEngine instance; stored in ibg-persist on flush.
  • On load, inner initialized to persisted_value + 1 to ensure monotonicity even after crash.
  • bump() called once per build wave start; all last_verified writes in that wave use the bumped value.

11.18 ResourceBudget

struct ResourceBudget {
    cpu_slots:     usize,              // max concurrent `ibg-exec` spawns
    memory_bytes:  Option<u64>,        // advisory; enforced by cgroup if sandbox active
    io_weight:     Option<u32>,        // blkio weight hint (Linux only)
}
  • Injected into Scheduler at session start; immutable during session.
  • cpu_slots default = num_cpus::get().
  • memory_bytes enforcement is best-effort on platforms without cgroup support.

11.19 Scheduler and WorkerHandle

struct Scheduler {
    budget:      ResourceBudget,
    ready:       Arc<Mutex<BinaryHeap<ReadyNode>>>,    // max-heap by critical-path rank
    in_flight:   Arc<AtomicUsize>,
    workers:     Vec<WorkerHandle>,
    notify:      Arc<tokio::sync::Notify>,
}

struct ReadyNode {
    id:           NodeId,
    critical_rank: u64,               // longer path = higher priority
}

struct WorkerHandle {
    thread:  tokio::task::JoinHandle<()>,
    steal_q: Arc<SegQueue<NodeId>>,   // crossbeam SegQueue for work-stealing
}
  • ready queue is global; steal_q per worker for local work-stealing dequeue.
  • in_flight counter never exceeds budget.cpu_slots; backpressure via Notify await.
  • critical_rank computed by ibg-sched::critical_path over the frozen BuildGraph.

11.20 PersistDb Schema Version

const SCHEMA_VERSION: u32 = 1;

struct SchemaHeader {
    magic:   [u8; 4],    // b"IBG\0"
    version: u32,
    created_at_revision: Revision,
}
  • On open, magic and version checked; mismatch → PersistError::SchemaMismatch → full re-evaluation.
  • Migration path: version N → N+1 via registered MigrationFn table; run before normal open.
  • CRC32 over header + first 4 KB of node-state table checked on open; failure → CorruptState.

11.21 WatchEvent and InvalidationBatch

enum WatchEventKind { Created, Modified, Deleted, Renamed { from: PathBuf } }

struct WatchEvent {
    path: PathBuf,
    kind: WatchEventKind,
}

struct InvalidationBatch {
    events:       Vec<WatchEvent>,
    affected:     BTreeSet<NodeId>,    // resolved via node_idx + affected_set
    at_revision:  Revision,
}
  • ibg-watch accumulates events in debounce window (default 50 ms), then emits one InvalidationBatch.
  • affected computed by BuildGraph::affected_set(changed_nodes) over reverse-dep index.
  • at_revision is the GlobalRevision at debounce flush time; engine bumps before re-evaluating affected set.

11.22 RemoteCacheClient Trait

trait RemoteCacheClient: Send + Sync {
    fn find_missing(&self, digests: &[Digest]) -> BoxFuture<Result<Vec<Digest>, RemoteError>>;
    fn batch_upload(&self, objects: Vec<CasObject>) -> BoxFuture<Result<(), RemoteError>>;
    fn batch_download(&self, digests: &[Digest]) -> BoxFuture<Result<Vec<CasObject>, RemoteError>>;
    fn get_action_result(&self, key: &ActionKey) -> BoxFuture<Result<Option<CacheEntry>, RemoteError>>;
    fn update_action_result(&self, entry: CacheEntry) -> BoxFuture<Result<(), RemoteError>>;
}
  • REAPI-compatible; gRPC impl uses tonic + generated proto stubs.
  • All methods are async; called from tokio runtime via ibg-engine post-exec hook.
  • Timeout + retry policy configured at construction; not exposed in trait.

11.23 BuildMetrics

struct BuildMetrics {
    cache_hits:        AtomicU64,
    cache_misses:      AtomicU64,
    early_cutoffs:     AtomicU64,
    actions_executed:  AtomicU64,
    actions_failed:    AtomicU64,
    nodes_evaluated:   AtomicU64,
    critical_path_ms:  AtomicU64,    // set once at build end
}
  • Owned by ibg-telemetry; shared via Arc<BuildMetrics> across engine + cache + sched.
  • All fields AtomicU64; updated with Relaxed ordering on hot path; SeqCst on final read.
  • Exported as Prometheus counters/gauges via ibg-telemetry::metrics::export_prometheus(&metrics).

11.24 Type Compatibility and Stability Guarantees

Type Wire stability DB stability Semver
NodeId NOT stable across graph versions NOT stable internal
NodeKey stable (UTF-8 string) stable public
Digest stable (blake3, 32 bytes) stable public
ActionKey stable (Digest newtype) stable public
CacheEntry versioned (schema_version field) versioned public
NodeState versioned (schema_version check) versioned internal
IbgError non_exhaustive N/A public
BuildRequest / BuildResult JSON-stable N/A public
  • Public types follow semver; breaking changes require major version bump of ibg-core.
  • Internal types (NodeId, NodeState) may change across minor versions; never exposed via public API.
  • All serializable types derive serde::Serialize + serde::Deserialize; #[serde(deny_unknown_fields)] on storage types; #[serde(flatten)] for forward-compat on wire types.

12. Phase 0 — Skeleton — crates, core types, a tiny static action DAG; topo-evaluate it once unconditionally and run actions; print the order (no caching, no incrementality)

0.1 Objectives

  • Establish Cargo workspace: 14 crates (ibg-core … ibg-bench) in Cargo.toml; all compile clean on MSRV 1.78 (rust-toolchain.toml); ALL inter-crate API boundaries established even if bodies are todo!(), no circular deps (Cargo-enforced).
  • Define ALL canonical types from the spine in ibg-core (§11), complete not placeholder; ibg-graph implements a minimal static DAG (Source+Action nodes, typed edges, adjacency list, no cycle detection yet).
  • ibg-engine Phase-0 mode: unconditional topological walk (Kahn's), no memoization, no revision counters — runs every node every time; ibg-exec Phase-0 mode: spawn via std::process::Command, capture stdout/stderr/exit, no sandboxing.
  • ibg-cli driver: parse --target <NodeKey> from argv, build graph, run topo-eval, print execution order + per-node exit codes.
  • No caching, no incrementality, no persistence, no file watching, no sandbox — all deferred to later phases (§11 lists the exact type spine reused verbatim here).

0.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

Workspace (Cargo.toml): [workspace] members = [ibg-core, ibg-graph, ibg-fingerprint, ibg-engine, ibg-cache, ibg-exec, ibg-sched, ibg-watch, ibg-persist, ibg-sandbox, ibg-remote, ibg-telemetry, ibg-cli, ibg-bench]; resolver="2", edition="2021", rust-version="1.78"; [workspace.dependencies] pins blake3=1, crossbeam=0.8, dashmap=5, petgraph=0.6, serde{derive}, bincode=2, notify=6, tokio{full}, tracing, tracing-subscriber, anyhow, thiserror, tempfile, criterion, proptest.

ibg-core/src/lib.rs (full type catalogue in §11 Core Schema and Core Types) exports: identifiers NodeId(u32), Revision(u64), Digest([u8;32]), NodeKey{kind_tag,path_or_label:Arc<str>}; NodeKind{Source{path},Action(ActionId),Glob{pattern},Group}; BuildNode{id,key,kind,deps,declared_outputs}; ActionId(u32) + Action{id,command,inputs,outputs,env,key}; Cmd{program,args,cwd}; EnvMap=BTreeMap<OsString,OsString> (sorted, deterministic hashing — P6); ActionKey(Digest); Edge{from,to,kind:EdgeKind{Static,Dynamic}}; NodeState{last_verified,last_changed,output_digest,status}; NodeStatus{Clean,Dirty,Running,Failed}; stub structs CacheEntry/CasObject/DynamicDep/ExecMeta (todo!() bodies, compiling); BuildRequest{targets}, BuildResult{built,cached,skipped,errors}; IbgError (thiserror enum): GraphCycle, NodeNotFound(NodeId), ActionFailed{id,exit_code}, IoError(#[from]), PersistCorrupt, SandboxViolation, CacheIo, RemoteUnavailable.

ibg-graph/src/lib.rs Phase-0 scope: BuildGraph{nodes:SlotMap<NodeId,BuildNode>, edges:Vec<Edge>, adj:HashMap<NodeId,Vec<NodeId>>} (outbound only); add_source(path)->NodeId, add_action(action)->NodeId, add_edge(from,to,kind); topo_sort()->Result<Vec<NodeId>,IbgError> — Kahn's BFS, Err(GraphCycle) on cycle (stub; full cycle detection lands in Phase 1, modeled on Salsa's approach, P3).

ibg-engine/src/phase0.rs: eval_unconditional(graph,request,exec:&dyn Executor)->BuildResult walks topo_sort() order, calls exec.run(&action)->ExecResult per Action node, prints [{idx}] {node_key} — ok|failed(exit={n}) to stdout.

ibg-exec/src/lib.rs: trait Executor: Send+Sync { fn run(&self, action:&Action)->Result<ExecResult,IbgError>; }; ExecResult{stdout,stderr,exit_code,output_digests}; ProcessExecutor wraps std::process::Command — serial in Phase 0, no sandbox, no CAS capture (sandboxing lands Phase 11, CAS capture lands Phase 5).

ibg-cli/src/main.rs: parses --target <label> (repeatable) + --graph-fixture <path> (JSON fixture); loads fixture → BuildGraph/BuildRequest → eval_unconditional → prints BuildResult; exit 0 if no errors else 1. ibg-telemetry/src/lib.rs: stub init_tracing() (tracing-subscriber env-filter).

Tests: ibg-graph/tests/phase0_topo.rs — 3-node chain A→B→C (topo=[A,B,C] or valid linearization), diamond A→B,A→C,B→D,C→D (D last); ibg-core/tests/types_compile.rs constructs one of each canonical type, asserts non-empty Debug output.

0.3 Deliverable

  • cargo build --workspace zero warnings (#![deny(warnings)] every crate root); cargo test --workspace passes all Phase-0 tests; cargo doc --workspace --no-deps succeeds (all 14 crates documented).
  • ibg-cli --graph-fixture fixtures/phase0_triangle.json --target C prints topo order + executes 3 echo actions in DAG order, exits 0; Cargo dep graph: ibg-core ← everything, ibg-graph ← ibg-engine, no cycles.

0.4 Acceptance Criteria

Check Pass condition
Compile + doc cargo build --workspace / cargo doc --no-deps zero errors/warnings, MSRV 1.78
Type coverage All 14 canonical types from §11 instantiable in unit tests
Topo sort 3-node chain, diamond, 10-node random DAG — all valid linearizations
CLI + dep graph --graph-fixture phase0_triangle.json runs 3 echo-actions in topo order exit 0; cargo tree shows no circular deps, ibg-core zero workspace deps
Exec + error path ExecResult.exit_code=0 for echo actions; empty graph → BuildResult{built:0,errors:[]}

0.5 Conformance Fixtures

Fixture Purpose Expected output
fixtures/phase0_triangle.json 3-node chain A→B→C, action=echo <label> topo order A B C, all exit 0
fixtures/phase0_diamond.json Diamond A→{B,C}→D D runs last; either B/C order; all exit 0
fixtures/phase0_single.json One source node, no actions BuildResult{built:0}
fixtures/phase0_bad_exit.json Action C = exit 1 errors contains ActionFailed{id:C,exit_code:1}
fixtures/phase0_types_roundtrip.json serde_json round-trip BuildNode/Action byte-identical re-encode

Malformed: phase0_unknown_dep.json (nonexistent NodeId ref) → IbgError::NodeNotFound; empty targets → BuildResult{built:0}.

0.6 Pitfalls

  • NodeId reuse after removal: SlotMap generational indices prevent stale-ID misreads (naive HashMap<u32,…> would let a removed+re-added node reuse an ID → silent wrong-node lookup, signal: spurious NodeNotFound); fix is the slotmap crate or an embedded generation field.
  • BTreeMap vs HashMap for EnvMap: HashMap iteration order is non-deterministic → ActionKey hash differs run-to-run for the same logical action → false cache misses later; invariant enforced by the EnvMap=BTreeMap type alias (the ActionKey Uniqueness invariant, §7).
  • Kahn's BFS mutable in-degree state: shared across concurrent calls → data race; Phase 0 is serial, but Phase 7's parallel scheduler must clone or snapshot immutably.
  • std::process::Command cwd not inherited: Cmd::cwd=None inherits the CLI process cwd, so actions assuming workspace root fail if invoked from a subdirectory; fix is canonicalizing cwd at CLI entry and passing it explicitly to ProcessExecutor.
  • Rustdoc #![deny(warnings)] + todo!(): emits "unreachable" warnings on stub impls; use #[allow(unreachable_code)] or typed panic strings instead of todo!().
  • Fixture JSON NodeId as u32 vs string: hand-edited string keys silently deserialize to 0 under serde_json; fix is #[serde(transparent)] on NodeId plus a fixture schema-validation test step.

0.7 Performance Concerns

  • Phase 0 is intentionally serial/non-optimized — no budgets apply yet; Vec<Edge> linear-scan adjacency is acceptable ≤1000 nodes, switch to HashMap<NodeId,Vec<NodeId>> before Phase 1.
  • SlotMap pre-allocates with_capacity(1024) to avoid rehash on test graphs; Command::spawn+wait is synchronous (no thread pool), setting the Phase-6 scheduler comparison baseline.
  • Avoid cloning OsString args in the hot path; migrate Cmd to Arc<[OsString]> by Phase 3.

0.8 Edge Cases

  • Empty targets: BuildRequest{targets:[]} → immediate BuildResult{built:0,cached:0,skipped:0,errors:[]}; single-node source-only graph: topo=[src], engine skips exec, built:0.
  • Action with 0 declared inputs: valid, still runs (unconditional in Phase 0); NodeId u32 overflow: undefined behavior documented in rustdoc, panic in debug / wrap in release, debug_assert on insert.
  • Unicode paths in Cmd::args: OsString is OS-native, no UTF-8 assumption (fixtures/phase0_unicode_path.json); action stdout > 64 KB: Command::output() buffers in memory in Phase 0 (Phase 5 streams to CAS instead).

0.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Detection Blast radius Recovery
GraphCycle in topo_sort Err(GraphCycle) Full build aborted Fix fixture; no state written
ActionFailed{exit_code} ExecResult.exit_code != 0 Downstream topo actions not run CLI exits 1; error logged
NodeNotFound(id) Adjacency lookup fails Build aborted Fix graph fixture
Command::spawn IO error IbgError::IoError Single action fails CLI reports; remaining actions skipped
cargo build cycle Cargo compile-time error Workspace broken Remove circular dep in Cargo.toml

Containment: Phase 0 has no persistent state to corrupt — all state in-memory; any panic → process exit → clean slate.

0.10 Operational Notes

  • tracing::info! at engine entry (phase0_eval targets={n} nodes={n}) and tracing::debug! per action (running action id={id} cmd={cmd:?}); RUST_LOG=ibg=debug enables verbose topo+exec trace.
  • No metrics exported in Phase 0 (ibg-telemetry::init_tracing() only sets up the env-filter subscriber); CLI --help/--version document flags and emit CARGO_PKG_VERSION.
  • Fixture JSON format is additive-stable across phases — Phase-0 fixtures remain valid regression baselines through Phase 1+; cargo test --workspace is the only CI gate in Phase 0 (no bench, no fuzz).

13. Phase 1 — Build graph model — nodes (source/action/glob), typed edges, declared inputs/outputs, DAG construction, cycle detection, topological + reverse-dependency indexing

1.1 Objectives

  • Complete ibg-graph: production DAG with Source/Action/Glob/Group kinds; typed edges (Static at construction, Dynamic at eval time via DynamicDep), both enforced in adjacency structures.
  • Declared inputs/outputs: every Action carries resolved inputs:Vec<NodeId> + declared outputs:Vec<PathBuf>; cycle detection is DFS-based O(V+E), run per-add_edge (incremental) or at explicit validate(), mode selected via CycleCheckMode (the Cycle Freedom invariant, §7).
  • Topological index (cached Vec<NodeId>, Kahn's BFS, invalidated on structural mutation) and reverse-dependency index (rev_deps: HashMap<NodeId,Vec<NodeId>>, built at construction, consumed by ibg-watch in Phase 9) and key_map: HashMap<NodeKey,NodeId> for CLI --target resolution.
  • Glob nodes: pattern → Vec<NodeId> expansion via a GlobExpander trait (default impl uses the glob crate).
  • Architectural constraint: graph is immutable after seal(); dynamic-dep additions go through a separate DynDepRegistry, never graph mutation, to avoid locking the immutable core (§7.11).

1.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

ibg-graph/src/graph.rs: BuildGraph{nodes:SlotMap<NodeId,BuildNode>, edges:Vec<Edge>, adj:HashMap<NodeId,Vec<NodeId>> (outbound static), rev:HashMap<NodeId,Vec<NodeId>> (inbound), key_map:HashMap<NodeKey,NodeId>, topo_cache:OnceCell<Vec<NodeId>>, sealed:bool}. add_node(kind,declared_outputs)->Result<NodeId,IbgError> derives NodeKey, inserts into nodes+key_map, asserts !sealed. add_edge(from,to,kind)->Result<(),IbgError> validates both IDs exist; under CycleCheckMode::Incremental runs DFS from to seeking from (O(V+E) worst case); appends adj[from]/rev[to]; invalidates topo_cache. seal(&mut self) sets sealed=true, runs full DFS cycle check (Salsa-style memoized traversal, P3), computes+caches topo order, builds rev index, computes fan-in/fan-out stats. topo_sorted() panics if unsealed else returns cached slice; reverse_deps(id) O(1) into rev; resolve_key(key), node(id).

ibg-graph/src/cycle.rs: detect_cycle_dfs(adj,nodes)->Option<Vec<NodeId>> (White/Gray/Black coloring) returns the first cycle as a node list; IbgError::GraphCycle carries Vec<NodeKey> for human-readable reporting.

ibg-graph/src/glob.rs: trait GlobExpander{fn expand(pattern,root)->Result<Vec<PathBuf>,IbgError>;}; DefaultGlobExpander (glob crate, sorted+dedup'd); add_glob_node(pattern,root,expander) expands → source nodes, creates a Glob node with edges to each.

ibg-graph/src/dyndep.rs: DynDepRegistry{RwLock<HashMap<NodeId,Vec<DynamicDep>>>}; add_dynamic_dep(dependent,dependency,discovered_at), deps_of(id)->Vec<DynamicDep>; graph stays immutable post-seal(), dynamic deps overlaid at eval time.

ibg-graph/src/stats.rs: GraphStats{node_count,edge_count,max_fan_in,max_fan_out,max_depth,source_count,action_count,glob_count}; BuildGraph::stats() computed once at seal().

Tests (ibg-graph/tests/): test_cycle_self_loop/test_cycle_two_node/test_cycle_three_node (all → Err(GraphCycle), 3-node case carries cycle path); test_topo_chain/test_topo_diamond/test_topo_wide_fan (100-node fan-in); test_rev_deps (diamond: rev[D]={B,C}, rev[B]={A}); test_seal_idempotent; test_glob_expansion (mock 3-path expander → 3 source nodes + glob node + 3 edges); test_dyndep_registry (4-thread Arc<DynDepRegistry> safety); proptest random DAG (100 nodes/300 edges via rejection sampling) → validate() always Ok.

1.3 Deliverable

  • ibg-graph API usable from ibg-engine/ibg-cli; cargo test -p ibg-graph passes all tests incl. proptest; --graph-fixture fixtures/phase1_large.json loads 1000-node graph, seals, prints GraphStats to stderr.
  • BuildGraph documented with invariants in rustdoc; cycle error messages include the full human-readable cycle path.

1.4 Acceptance Criteria

Check Pass condition
Cycle detection Self-loop, 2-node, 3-node, 10-node cycle all → Err(GraphCycle)
Topo + rev-dep correctness Every edge (u→v): u before v in topo order; diamond rev[D]={B,C} exactly
Seal immutability + glob add_node after seal() → Err(GraphSealed); pattern *.c over 10 files → 10 source nodes
GraphStats + Proptest Diamond fan_in=2 at D; 1000 random-DAG iterations, no panics/false cycles
1000-node seal + key lookup seal() < 50 ms wall; all inserted nodes resolvable by key, unknown → None

1.5 Conformance Fixtures

Fixture Purpose Expected
fixtures/phase1_chain_100.json 100-node chain topo = linear; max_depth=100
fixtures/phase1_diamond.json A→{B,C}→D rev[D]={B,C}, topo D last
fixtures/phase1_cycle_self.json / _cycle_3.json A→A / A→B→C→A Err(GraphCycle); 3-node error includes [A,B,C]
fixtures/phase1_glob_mock.json Glob node, 5-path mock expander 5 source nodes + 1 glob node
fixtures/phase1_dyndep.json 2 static nodes + 1 dynamic dep DynDepRegistry returns 1 dep
fixtures/phase1_large_1000.json 1000 nodes, ~3000 edges (acyclic) seal() < 50 ms, stats accurate
fixtures/phase1_unknown_dep.json Edge references non-existent NodeId Err(NodeNotFound)

Malformed: empty declared_outputs on an action node is valid (may produce none); NodeKey collision → Err(DuplicateKey).

1.6 Pitfalls

  • DFS cycle check on incremental add_edge: O(V+E) per call, O(E·(V+E)) cumulative on large graphs; mitigate with CycleCheckMode::Deferred (check only at seal()), signaled via tracing::info!("cycle_check_ms=…").
  • OnceCell topo cache invalidation: add_edge after topo_sorted() without re-seal() serves stale topo; fix is topo_cache:Option<Vec<NodeId>> reset to None on every add_edge, and topo_sorted() panics unless sealed.
  • HashMap non-deterministic adjacency order: two runs of the same DAG can yield different (both valid) topo orders, flaking exact-order tests; fix with IndexMap or sorted adjacency, and assert validity not exact equality.
  • Glob expansion race: DefaultGlobExpander snapshots the filesystem at construction time; files added after are stale until ibg-watch (Phase 9) re-expands on the relevant FS event.
  • DynDepRegistry cycle via dynamic edge: a dynamic dep closing a cycle deadlocks the scheduler; add_dynamic_dep validates against sealed-graph reachability and rejects with GraphCycle (full dynamic-cycle detection is Phase 8's concern, per the Cycle Freedom invariant in §7).
  • SlotMap generational overflow: u32 space (2^32) is safe for any realistic graph; guarded by debug_assert!(nodes.len() < u32::MAX as usize). seal() on empty graph: valid, Ok(()), topo_sorted()=&[].

1.7 Performance Concerns

  • seal() topo sort (Kahn's) O(V+E), target <10 ms at 10K nodes/50K edges; reverse-dep index built O(E) in one pass, no per-edge allocation beyond the HashMap push.
  • adj/rev as HashMap<NodeId,Vec<NodeId>> may switch to Vec<Vec<NodeId>> (indexed by dense NodeId.0) for O(1) access behind a feature flag; GraphStats::max_depth is a BFS from all sources, O(V+E), cached at seal().
  • NodeKey hashing must hash content (no Arc pointer-equality shortcut) — use fnv/ahash; glob expansion I/O is synchronous in Phase 1, consider rayon parallel expansion past 10K files (Phase 1.5).

1.8 Edge Cases

  • Zero-node graph: seal() succeeds, topo_sorted()=&[], stats all-zero; all-source graph: valid, Phase-0 engine mode runs nothing; isolated NodeId: adj.get(id)=None, appears in topo unconstrained.
  • Duplicate add_edge(A,B): idempotent by design (simpler than fragile caller-side tracking; documented in API); very wide fan-in (1 action, 10K inputs): Kahn's in-degree handles correctly, ~40 KB memory for IDs.
  • Symlinks in declared_outputs: canonicalized and resolved at construction time; Group node: edges to children only, no Cmd, engine skips execution, used for --target group_label.

1.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Detection Blast radius Recovery
GraphCycle at seal() Err with cycle path Full build aborted Fix graph inputs; no state persisted
DuplicateKey / NodeNotFound in add_edge Err at add_node/add_edge Node/edge not inserted Caller deduplicates / adds nodes before edges
Glob expander I/O error Err(IoError) Glob node not created CLI reports; build aborted
DynDepRegistry cycle Err(GraphCycle) at add_dynamic_dep Dynamic dep rejected Action re-runs without dep; error logged

1.10 Operational Notes

  • tracing::info! at seal() (graph_sealed nodes={} edges={} max_depth={} max_fan_in={}); tracing::warn! if max_fan_in>1000 (perf concern for downstream hashing); GraphStats also emitted as structured log.
  • ibg-telemetry hook on_graph_sealed(stats) is a placeholder for future metrics export; fixture files under ibg-graph/fixtures/ are version-controlled JSON with "schema_version":1.
  • cargo test -p ibg-graph -- --nocapture prints topo orders for manual inspection; no persistent state in Phase 1 — graph lives only in memory per invocation.

14. Phase 2 — Fingerprinting + change detection — file content digests (blake3), stat-based fast path (mtime/size/inode), action key = hash(command + input digests + env), digest cache

2.1 Objectives

  • Implement ibg-fingerprint: complete content-hash + change-detection subsystem, with NO dependency on ibg-graph/ibg-engine (pure file+hash+cache, §6.12 Minimal Coupling).
  • file_digest(path)->Result<Digest,IbgError> (blake3, chunked streaming); stat fast-path StatEntry{mtime,size,inode,dev} skips re-hash when all fields match a cached entry (P3).
  • DigestCache: in-memory HashMap<PathBuf,(StatEntry,Digest)> behind RwLock, persisted by ibg-persist in Phase 9; action_key(action,input_digests)->ActionKey hashes command||sorted_env||sorted input_digests (P6, Bazel ActionKey model).
  • is_dirty(path,cached_stat,cached_digest)->Dirtiness{StatMismatch|ContentChanged|Clean} drives engine decisions (StatMismatch→re-hash, ContentChanged→propagate dirty, Clean→early-cutoff candidate).
  • tool_digest(path)->Result<Digest,IbgError> hashes the action's tool binary, folded into ActionKey to detect tool upgrades.

2.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

hash.rs: file_digest(path) streams 64 KB chunks into blake3::Hasher, finalize()→Digest (IoError on any read failure); bytes_digest(data)=blake3::hash; hasher_feed_cmd(h,cmd) feeds length-prefixed program bytes, arg count + len-prefixed args, and cwd as Option<&[u8]> with a presence byte (collision-safe framing per BLAKE3's keyed-mode domain separation, P14).

stat.rs: StatEntry{mtime_ns:i128,size,inode,dev} via std::fs::Metadata; stat_entry(path) wraps fs::metadata; on Windows inode=dev=0 (fast-path degrades to mtime+size, documented); stat_matches(cached,fresh) requires all fields equal.

cache.rs: DigestCache{inner:RwLock<HashMap<PathBuf,CacheEntry{stat,digest,revision}>>}. get_or_compute(path,current_rev)->Result<(Digest,Freshness),IbgError>: read-lock lookup; if found and stat_matches → return (cached.digest,Fresh); else drop lock, file_digest, write-lock insert/update, return (digest,Recomputed) — Freshness{Fresh,Recomputed} feeds telemetry counters. invalidate(path) (write-lock remove, called by ibg-watch on FS events), invalidate_all() (schema migration/corruption), snapshot()/restore() for persistence round-trip.

action_key.rs: compute_action_key(action,input_digests)->ActionKey feeds Cmd via hasher_feed_cmd, then sorted BTreeMap env pairs (len(k)+k+len(v)+v), then input count + sorted (NodeId.0,digest.0) pairs, then optional tool_digest, then finalize(). Invariant: BTreeMap env + sorted input pairs guarantee identical ActionKey regardless of insertion order (the ActionKey Uniqueness invariant, §7).

dirty.rs: Dirtiness{Clean, StatMismatch{path}, ContentChanged{path,old,new}}; check_dirtiness(path,cached_stat,cached_digest): stat match→Clean; else re-hash, digest match→update stat cache→Clean (touched, same content); else→ContentChanged. is_missing(path) treats permission errors as missing (logged warn).

lib.rs: Fingerprinter aggregates DigestCache, exposing the unified API ibg-engine consumes.

Tests: stability (test_digest_stable, _content_change), stat fast-path (file_digest invoked exactly once on repeat call), ActionKey determinism across 100 computations and env/input reordering, dirtiness transitions (Clean/ContentChanged/touched-only), 8-thread get_or_compute concurrency (no deadlock, consistent digest); bench: file_digest on 10 MB, get_or_compute cache-hit throughput.

2.3 Deliverable

  • cargo test -p ibg-fingerprint all passing; ibg-cli --digest <path> prints blake3:<hex>; DigestCache usable via Arc<Fingerprinter>; ActionKey verified deterministic across processes.
  • Bench baseline: cache-hit get_or_compute <1 µs; cache-miss (10 MB) <50 ms.

2.4 Acceptance Criteria

Check Pass condition
Digest stability + stat fast-path Same file → same Digest over 1000 calls; cache-hit never invokes file_digest (mock-verified)
ActionKey determinism 100 computations + reversed env/input order → identical ActionKey
ContentChanged + concurrency 1-byte change detected <1 ms (1 KB file); 8-thread test: no panic/deadlock, consistent result
Cache-hit latency + throughput get_or_compute hit <1 µs median; file_digest 100 MB <500 ms (~2 GB/s)
invalidate correctness After invalidate(path), next get_or_compute re-hashes

2.5 Conformance Fixtures

Fixture Purpose Expected
fixtures/fp_stable_file.txt Known content, hardcoded expected digest blake3:abc123… exact hex
fixtures/fp_action_key_ref.json Reference Action + input digests Deterministic hex, cross-platform
fixtures/fp_env_variants.json Same KV pairs, different insertion order Same ActionKey
fixtures/fp_large_10mb.bin 10 MB random bytes Consistent digest; bench baseline
fixtures/fp_touched_file.txt touched; stat changed, content same Dirtiness::Clean after re-hash
fixtures/fp_missing_file.json / fp_symlink.txt Non-existent path / symlink to stable file Err(IoError) / digest of symlink TARGET

Malformed: empty file → valid 32-byte digest (not an error); directory path → IoError; null-byte binary → valid (blake3 is byte-agnostic).

2.6 Pitfalls

  • mtime resolution: FAT32/HFS+ 1–2s granularity lets two same-second writes share an mtime and return a stale cached digest; fix by also comparing size (force re-hash on mismatch regardless of mtime); modern Linux ext4/btrfs is nanosecond-precise.
  • Symlink vs target digest: policy is always follow symlinks (digest target content); a retarget to same-content file legitimately reports Clean under content-addressable semantics.
  • TOCTOU (file deleted between stat and read): stat_entry succeeds then file_digest gets IoError; treat as ContentChanged (gone = changed), propagate IoError so the engine marks the node Dirty.
  • RwLock write contention: 8+ threads hashing distinct files serialize on the single write-lock insert; mitigate via a 16-shard ShardedDigestCache (path-hash sharded, API-transparent).
  • ActionKey cross-platform non-determinism: normalize all PathBufs to forward-slash strings before hashing (Path::to_string_lossy().replace('\\','/')) so Windows/Unix agree.
  • Silent tool-binary changes: tool_digest=None → tool upgrade keeps the same ActionKey → stale hit; callers MUST set tool_digest, validated at the ibg-sandbox layer (Phase 10). blake3 SIMD: runtime AVX2/NEON detection with scalar fallback — correct either way, no action needed.

2.7 Performance Concerns

  • file_digest 64 KB chunking matches L1/L2 + IO-scheduler optimum, ≥2 GB/s on NVMe; DigestCache reads (RwLock::read()) allow concurrent readers, contention only on write (16-shard design drops it 16×).
  • action_key is in-memory blake3::Hasher ops, <1 µs for typical (<100-input) actions, no allocation beyond hasher state; stat_entry is one stat(2) syscall (~1 µs cold, ~100 ns dcache-hot) — 10K inputs ≈10 ms, acceptable.
  • snapshot() clones the whole HashMap (~13 MB at 100K entries) — fine at a build-end checkpoint, never on the hot path; hasher_feed_cmd length-prefixes every variable field (one extra u64 write) to prevent concatenation collisions.

2.8 Edge Cases

  • Empty file: blake3 of zero bytes is a valid deterministic digest, size=0; size changes, mtime static (O_DIRECT/FAT32): size mismatch always forces re-hash independent of mtime.
  • File >4 GB: u64 size handles it, streaming read avoids OOM, blake3 tree-hashes internally in parallel; concurrent write+read: partial-content digest is caught by re-hash on next invocation (documented as eventual consistency within a build window).
  • Revision u64 overflow: ~584B years at 1 build/sec — no runtime check needed; corrupt DigestCache snapshot: malformed bincode → PersistCorrupt → invalidate_all() → full re-hash.
  • Windows (no inode): StatEntry{inode:0,dev:0}, stat_matches falls back to mtime+size, degraded but correct; hardlinked files: independent per-path cache entries, identical digests, no special handling.

2.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Detection Blast radius Recovery
file_digest I/O error Err(IoError) Node marked Dirty/Failed Retries next build if file restored
Stat fast-path false-negative Extra re-hashes only Slower build, no wrong result Alert if Recomputed rate >5%
ActionKey cross-platform mismatch Differing key Win vs Linux Remote cache misses Path normalization; fp_action_key_ref.json
DigestCache deadlock / corrupt snapshot Hang w/ RUST_LOG=trace / PersistCorrupt Build stall / full re-hash Panic-on-poison + investigate / invalidate_all()
Tool digest unset → stale hit Wrong cached output served silently Incorrect artifact Sandbox validation (Phase 11); audit tool_digest setting

Postmortem trigger: cache_hit_rate drop >20% with no source changes → investigate invalidation or ActionKey non-determinism.

2.10 Operational Notes

  • tracing::debug! per call (fingerprint path={:?} freshness={:?} digest={:?}, 1% sampled); tracing::warn! when stat-miss rate >10% in a build.
  • Metrics: fingerprint_cache_hits/misses, fingerprint_bytes_hashed, fingerprint_stat_fast_path (all Counters, ibg-telemetry); --digest <path> CLI prints blake3:<hex> mtime=<ns> size=<bytes>.
  • DigestCache snapshot is bincode v2 of HashMap<PathBuf,(StatEntry,Digest)> with a leading schema-version u32; shard count (16) is the compile-time DIGEST_CACHE_SHARDS constant, tunable for benchmarking.
  • cargo bench -p ibg-bench --bench fingerprint commits baselines to benches/baselines/; on SIGINT/SIGTERM the cache is not flushed (persistence is Phase 9) — conservative, next build re-hashes changed files.

15. Phase 3 — Demand-driven incremental engine — pull-based evaluation, memoization, dirty/verified state, revision counters (Salsa red/green), recompute-only-if-key-changed

3.1 Objectives

Goal Constraint
Pull-based eval loop in ibg-engine, Salsa-style red/green Revision counters per node Drives only nodes reachable from BuildRequest targets; global Revision(u64) monotone, per-node last_verified/last_changed
Dirty/verified state machine Clean|Dirty|Running|Failed Transitions lock-protected; no silent state skips (§6.15)
Memoization (skip eval when last_verified==current_revision) + recompute gate (re-exec only on ActionKey change) Fingerprint before scheduling — avoids spurious executor calls
Async-capable eval frame with cycle-in-dynamic-dep detection Cycle → CyclicDynamicDep; never deadlocks
Interface to ibg-graph (topo, reverse edges) + ibg-fingerprint (digest) No direct FS I/O inside engine

Scope: Phase 3 wires graph+fingerprint+state machine (Salsa's red/green model, P3); cache/exec land Phases 4–5.

3.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

lib.rs: Engine::new(graph,fingerprint,persist,telemetry)->Engine; Engine::evaluate(req:BuildRequest)->Result<BuildResult,IbgError>; Engine::mark_dirty(node,at:Revision).

eval.rs (core pull loop): EvalCtx carries current_revision + a thread-local in-flight HashSet<NodeId> for cycle detection. eval_node(node_id,ctx)->NodeOutcome recursively: locks NodeState, returns memoized output_digest if last_verified==current_revision; else CAS Dirty→Running, recursively pulls declared static deps, computes ActionKey via ibg-fingerprint::action_key; unchanged key → last_verified=current_revision,status=Clean, early return (no exec, P3); changed key → dispatches to ExecSlot (stubbed here, wired Phase 6), then on completion records output_digest+bumps last_changed/last_verified. In-flight set checked before recursing (contains(id)→CyclicDynamicDep).

state_store.rs: NodeStateStore=DashMap<NodeId,NodeState> (per-shard striping); bump_revision()=AtomicU64::fetch_add(1,SeqCst); mark_dirty(ids,rev) walks the reverse-dep closure from ibg-graph::reverse_index, setting status=Dirty,last_changed=rev.

revision.rs: global REVISION:AtomicU64 + current_revision(); invariant — never wraps (overflow → RevisionOverflow, >10^18 builds impossible in practice; the Revision Monotonicity invariant, §7).

Persistence bridge: post-eval persist.commit_node_states(snapshot) batch-writes dirtied nodes; startup persist.load_node_states() hydrates NodeStateStore (Phase 10). Telemetry: NodeEvalStart{node_id,revision}/NodeEvalEnd{outcome,duration_us} via tracing::span!+ibg-telemetry::emit.

Tests: test_memoization_skip (second build touches 0 exec slots), test_dirty_propagation (only dependents of a mutated source re-evaluate), test_cycle_detected (circular dynamic dep → CyclicDynamicDep), test_revision_monotone (10k sequential evals strictly increasing).

3.3 Deliverable

  • ibg-engine compiles against ibg-graph/ibg-fingerprint/ibg-persist stubs; Engine::evaluate runs end-to-end on a 1000-node synthetic graph with correct topo traversal + correct memoization skip on a no-change second call.
  • ibg-bench 10k-node/0-change scenario dispatches 0 exec slots, eval <50 ms; tracing spans visible under RUST_LOG=ibg_engine=debug; ibg-persist stub round-trips NodeState snapshots (hash-verified).

3.4 Acceptance Criteria

Criterion Threshold
Memoization + dirty propagation 0 exec dispatches unchanged graph; only affected subgraph re-evaluates
Cycle detection + revision monotonicity CyclicDynamicDep in <5 ms no deadlock; revision strictly increasing over 10k evals
10k-node no-change eval <50 ms wall, <20 MB heap delta
State hydration round-trip Byte-identical NodeState after persist+reload
cargo test/clippy -p ibg-engine 100% pass, no unsafe, no loom data races, zero clippy warnings

3.6 Pitfalls

Pitfall Manifestation Root cause Detection Recovery
Double-eval under lock drop Node executed twice concurrently Running not checked before 2nd thread enters loom; duplicate NodeEvalStart in trace CAS Dirty→Running; loser spin-waits on Running→Clean
Stale memoization after rollback Old digest served post-crash last_verified not cleared on corrupt load Persist checksum on load Force status=Dirty on checksum failure
Revision not propagated / cycle in static deps Dependents skip re-eval / stack overflow mark_dirty not transitive / graph-cycle bug test_dirty_propagation; cycle check missing Full reverse-dep BFS; run ibg-graph cycle check before engine entry
AtomicU8 status race / DashMap hot shard Dependent reads stale Clean / throughput collapse Ordering weaker than needed / skewed NodeId hash loom ordering test / shard-lock saturation AcqRel write + Acquire read / re-shard by NodeId % 256
In-flight set not cleared on error Later evals see false cycle HashSet not reset on CyclicDynamicDep path test_cycle_then_clean Wrap push/pop with scopeguard

3.7 Performance Concerns

  • Lock granularity: DashMap 64 shards, NodeId % 64; target <5% shard contention at 8 threads/10k nodes. **Recursion depth**: chains >10k risk stack overflow — use explicit VecDeque<NodeId> for topo traversal, reserve recursion for dynamic-dep resolution only.
  • Hot-path allocation: pre-allocate a thread-local dep-digest scratch Vec<Digest>, never allocate per eval_node call; Revision reads (Relaxed) are ~1 ns, negligible at 10k nodes.
  • mark_dirty BFS: O(E), batch dirty-marks by BFS level on 100k-node graphs to avoid repeated shard locks; DashMap beats Arc<Mutex<HashMap>> at ≥4 threads for the 80%-read memoization workload; tracing::span! per node (~200 ns) — gate behind RUST_LOG.

3.8 Edge Cases

Edge case Handling
Empty BuildRequest Immediate BuildResult{built:0,cached:0,skipped:0,errors:[]}
Source leaf (0 deps) Fingerprint read only, no recursive pull
Revision(u64::MAX) bump_revision()→RevisionOverflow, halt
Concurrent mark_dirty+eval_node CAS ordering prevents regressing Running→Dirty mid-eval
Persist load corrupt Discard all states, whole graph Dirty, WARN persist_corrupt
Unknown NodeId in store Initialized Dirty, last_verified=Revision(0)
Fan-in 1→1000 / fan-out 1→1000 outputs BFS visited-set dedupes; output digest = blake3 of sorted concatenated file digests

3.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Detection Blast radius Recovery Operator visibility
Engine panics mid-eval catch_unwind around eval_node Single target fails Retry next invocation InternalPanic; panic counter
Persist commit failure Err from commit_node_states States not durable Next invocation full re-eval WARN persist_commit_failed
Spurious Clean from hydration Under-build (correctness bug) Wrong result served Validate checksum+version on load; invalidate all ERROR persist_integrity_fail
ActionKey collision (birthday) Distinct inputs, same key Incorrect cache hit Theoretical at blake3-256; force re-exec on suspicion WARN actionkey_collision_possible; counter

3.10 Operational Notes

  • Metrics: engine_nodes_evaluated, _skipped_memo, _dirty, engine_revision_current; tracing::instrument on eval_node with node_id/revision/outcome fields.
  • Log levels: DEBUG per-node, INFO batch summary, WARN persist issues, ERROR panics/cycles; NodeState schema version bump discards old states (full rebuild, no in-place migration in Phase 3).
  • Rollback: delete persist DB → next eval is a safe full rebuild; engine is a library (no daemon), invoked per build; NodeId(u32) caps graphs at 4B nodes (future schema widens to u64 if ever needed).

16. Phase 4 — Early cutoff — when an action re-runs but produces an unchanged output digest, STOP propagation; dependents stay verified; the defining incremental-correctness optimization

4.1 Objectives

Goal Constraint
Early-cutoff check in eval.rs: after exec/cache-hit, compare new output_digest vs stored last_output_digest Unchanged → last_verified=current_revision, last_changed NOT bumped (dependents stay verified without re-eval)
Changed → bump last_changed, propagate dirty to dependents Downstream re-evals triggered lazily on next pull
No-under-build invariant: if output did change, propagation is total Any dependent caching this node's last_changed detects the bump
No-over-build invariant: dependents with last_verified >= dep.last_changed skip eval The verified check is the gate and must never be bypassed (the No-Over-Build invariant, §7)
digest_eq(a,b) constant-time compare (ibg-fingerprint) Prevents a timing oracle on digest comparison

Scope: modifies Phase 3's eval_node; no new crate; adds last_output_digest:Option<Digest> to NodeState.

4.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

ibg-core/src/types.rs: extends NodeState with last_output_digest: Option<Digest> — the digest recorded after the last successful exec, alongside the existing last_verified/last_changed/status fields (see the NodeState schema in §11).

ibg-engine/src/eval.rs: cutoff logic runs immediately after exec (or cache hit) yields new_digest. When Some(new_digest) equals the stored last_output_digest, this is the early-cutoff branch: set last_verified=current_revision and leave last_changed untouched, then emit NodeEarlyCutoff{node_id,revision} — dependents reading last_changed see no change and stay verified. Otherwise this is the output-changed branch: store the new digest, bump both last_changed and last_verified to current_revision, and emit NodeOutputChanged{node_id,old_digest,new_digest,revision}. The dependent-verification gate in eval_node, evaluated before recursing into each dep, skips recursion whenever dep.last_changed <= self.last_verified (the No-Over-Build invariant, §7) — this comparison, not exec, is what makes cutoff cheap.

ibg-fingerprint/src/lib.rs: digest_eq(a,b)->bool via subtle::ConstantTimeEq, avoiding a timing side channel on the 32-byte compare.

ibg-engine/src/propagation.rs (new): propagate_changed(node_id,rev,graph,state_store) BFS over reverse edges, marking each dependent Dirty only if last_verified < rev — lazy, marks only, never re-evals eagerly; invoked solely from the output-changed branch.

ibg-persist: NodeState serialization gains last_output_digest (32-byte Digest + 1-byte Option tag; columnar encoding keeps sequential write patterns, §10.7).

Tests (ibg-engine/tests/cutoff.rs): test_cutoff_no_propagation (same output → 0 downstream evals), test_cutoff_propagation_on_change (different output → all dependents re-eval), test_cutoff_chain (10-node chain, cutoff at node 2, nodes 3–10 skip), test_cutoff_correctness (hand-verified outputs post-cutoff).

4.3 Deliverable

  • ibg-engine with cutoff: a whitespace-only source edit yielding the same compiled artifact triggers 0 downstream rebuilds, proven by the engine_cutoffs telemetry counter.
  • ibg-bench 500-node scenario: change 1 leaf, cutoff at depth 3, only 3 of 497 nodes rebuild; tracing shows NodeEarlyCutoff events under RUST_LOG=ibg_engine=info; ibg-persist round-trips last_output_digest.

4.4 Acceptance Criteria

Criterion Threshold
No-propagation / propagation-on-change 0 downstream evals same-output; all N dependents re-eval on digest change
Chain cutoff depth + underbuild sweep Cutoff at correct depth, tail untouched; 0 failures over 1000-run randomized sweep
Cutoff path overhead <200 ns/node (digest compare + state update)
cargo test -p ibg-engine --test cutoff 100% pass; persist round-trip byte-identical after reload

4.5 Conformance Fixtures

Fixture Purpose Expected output
fixtures/cutoff/same_output.json / different_output.json Input changes, output identical / differs engine_cutoffs=1,0 downstream / engine_cutoffs=0, all re-eval
fixtures/cutoff/chain_cutoff_depth3.json 10-node chain, cutoff at node 3 Nodes 4–10 last_verified unchanged
fixtures/cutoff/no_prior_digest.json First build, last_output_digest=None No cutoff; full exec path
fixtures/cutoff/digest_corrupt.json Bit-flipped last_output_digest in persist Force re-exec (checksum mismatch)
fixtures/cutoff/concurrent_cutoff.json 8 threads, shared 200-node graph, mixed cutoff No races; correct last_changed per node
fixtures/cutoff/regression_underbuild.json Regression: cutoff when output DID change Must NOT cutoff; asserts re-eval

4.6 Pitfalls

Pitfall Manifestation Root cause Detection Recovery
Cutoff on first build (None) Action skipped entirely None==None evaluated as a match Option::is_some() missing Only apply cutoff when digest is_some()
Non-deterministic action → false cutoff Stale output served Bytes differ per run, second run matches by coincidence Reproducibility check (ibg-sandbox, Phase 9) Double-exec detection in sandbox mode
last_verified advanced without last_changed bump Dependents skip needed re-eval Cutoff branch taken erroneously test_cutoff_regression_underbuild Strict digest compare; log old/new on mismatch
Race: two threads exec same action Second overwrites with a possibly different digest No exec dedup Running state gate (Phase 3) Confirmed by loom; only one thread execs per node
Non-constant-time digest compare Timing leak Naive byte compare Code review / subtle audit digest_eq mandates subtle::ConstantTimeEq
Stale last_changed read Wrong gate decision Written without Release ordering loom ordering test Release write, Acquire read

4.7 Performance Concerns

  • Digest compare ~10 ns (32-byte constant-time), negligible vs exec latency; propagation BFS is O(E) — batch dirty-marks per level on 100k-node/high-fan-out graphs to avoid repeated shard-lock acquisitions.
  • last_output_digest storage: 32 bytes × N nodes (3.2 MB at 100k) — acceptable; persist write amplification mitigated via columnar digest encoding for sequential writes.
  • No-cutoff hot path (output always changes) adds ~50 ns/node (one compare + one Option check); target cutoff rate ≥60% on a 10k-node graph with 1% source churn in a well-factored build; mark the None-first-build path #[cold], the Some path #[inline(always)].

4.8 Edge Cases

Edge case Handling
Empty output (0 bytes) / directory-tree output Digest=blake3("") valid, cutoff applies normally / merkle root of sorted file digests compared as one Digest
last_output_digest from older schema version Version mismatch → treated as None → full exec
Previous run Failed, digest None No cutoff; re-exec, digest set on success
Two actions share a declared output path Rejected at ibg-graph validation (Phase 2); never reaches cutoff logic
Concurrent build of a dependent during cutoff Dependent thread sees last_changed < current_revision → correctly skips
Digest is all-zeros / orphaned in persist Compared normally, not a sentinel / ignored on load if node no longer in graph

4.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Detection Blast radius Recovery Operator visibility
Under-build: cutoff fires though output changed Persist checksum catches partial writes Wrong outputs served downstream Full rebuild via --force ERROR engine_underbuild_detected (audit mode)
Over-build: cutoff never fires Benchmark alert, engine_cutoffs=0 unexpectedly Perf regression only, no correctness issue Fix compare logic WARN engine_cutoff_rate_low
Cascade from cutoff miss in a chain Telemetry counter vs expected Build-time regression ibg-cli build --trace-cutoff Per-node cutoff decision in trace
Persist last_output_digest corrupted Per-node CRC32 mismatch Single node wrong cutoff decision Invalidate node; re-exec WARN persist_node_crc_fail node={id}

4.10 Operational Notes

  • Metrics: engine_cutoffs, engine_cutoff_rate, engine_nodes_propagated; tracing events NodeEarlyCutoff/NodeOutputChanged; audit mode (ibg-cli build --audit) re-execs every node against its stored digest to surface under-build candidates.
  • Log: INFO cutoff, DEBUG output-changed with old/new digest; adding last_output_digest bumps the ibg-persist schema (old DBs read as all-None → one full rebuild, then cutoff resumes); removing it on downgrade is safe (ignored field).
  • Operator guidance: cutoff rate <30% on a typical incremental build suggests action non-determinism or undeclared inputs — investigate with --audit.

17. Phase 5 — Action cache + content-addressed store — action cache (key -> result), CAS output store, cache hit skips execution, local backend, GC/eviction

5.1 Objectives

Goal Constraint
ibg-cache: action cache (ActionKey→CacheEntry) + CAS output store Local filesystem backend only in Phase 5; remote is Phase 11
Cache hit: look up ActionKey before exec dispatch, materialize outputs from CAS on hit ibg-exec is not invoked on a cache hit
CAS store content-addressed by Digest: put(digest,bytes)/get(digest)->bytes Atomic write (tmp+rename); no partial objects in store
GC/eviction: size-bounded, LRU by last-access Configurable max_size_bytes; runs async post-build
CacheEntry{key,outputs:Vec<Digest>,metadata:ExecMeta} (exit code, stdout/stderr digest, wall time) —
Cache-correctness invariant: a hit is valid IFF inputs were fully/correctly declared Undeclared inputs → sandbox poisons the entry (Phase 9)

Scope: ibg-cache/src/; integrates with ibg-engine (lookup before dispatch) and ibg-exec (store after exec).

5.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

lib.rs: Cache::new(config), lookup(key)->Result<Option<CacheEntry>,IbgError>, insert(key,entry), get_object(digest)->Result<Bytes,IbgError>, put_object(digest,data), gc(policy)->Result<GcReport,IbgError>.

action_cache.rs: default backend is a flat-file index at $IBG_CACHE_DIR/action_cache.bin (bincode HashMap<ActionKey,CacheEntry>, memory-mapped); lookup deserializes the entry and verifies every output Digest exists in CAS before returning Some (dangling entries return None, forcing re-exec); insert writes to a tmp file, fsyncs, and renames atomically. In-process RwLock<ActionCacheIndex> plus a file-level flock guard multi-process safety.

cas.rs: directory layout cas/<hex[0..2]>/<hex[2..4]>/<full_hex> (256-way sharded); put_object writes .tmp, fsyncs, renames; get_object reads to Bytes and (in paranoid mode) re-verifies blake3(data)==digest; exists(digest) is a metadata()-only fast path; concurrent puts of the same digest are idempotent since rename is atomic.

gc.rs: GcPolicy{max_size_bytes,min_age_secs}; walks the CAS dir sorted by atime ascending, deletes oldest until under budget, then purges action-cache entries whose outputs no longer exist; runs on a background thread post-build (never blocks eval); emits GcReport{bytes_freed,objects_removed,entries_purged}.

ibg-engine integration (Adapton's demand-driven check-before-recompute model, P4): before exec dispatch, the engine looks up action_key in the cache; a hit materializes outputs from CAS via ibg-exec::materialize_outputs (each (digest,path) pair read from CAS, written to path, permissions set, digest re-verified in paranoid mode), emits CacheHit{key,node_id}, and returns the aggregate output digest without touching the executor; a miss proceeds to exec and, on completion, inserts the new CacheEntry into the action cache.

config.rs: CacheConfig{dir,max_size_bytes,paranoid_verify,gc_trigger_ratio}; defaults max_size_bytes=10 GiB, paranoid_verify=false, gc_trigger_ratio=0.9.

Tests: test_cache_hit/_miss (lookup returns Some/None correctly), test_cas_roundtrip (put/get byte-identical), test_cas_atomic_write (simulated crash mid-write leaves no corrupt object), test_gc_eviction (110%-full store → post-GC size ≤ max), test_cache_hit_skips_exec (pre-populated cache, exec slot never invoked).

5.3 Deliverable

  • ibg-cache compiles; lookup/insert + CAS put/get functional; ibg-engine integration shows non-zero cache_hits on a no-change second build.
  • ibg-bench 1000-action graph: cold first build, 100%-hit second build <200 ms (materialize only); GC runs post-build with a bounded store; ls cas/ shows the sharded tree, action_cache.bin exists.

5.4 Acceptance Criteria

Criterion Threshold
Cache hit/miss + CAS round-trip Correct Some/None; byte-identical retrieval
Atomic write + GC eviction No corrupt object after simulated crash; store ≤ max_size_bytes post-GC
Cache-hit-skips-exec Exec slot call count = 0 on full hit
Cold→warm speedup (1000 actions) ≥10× faster
Lookup / CAS-put latency <500 µs p99 lookup (mmap); <10 ms p99 put (1 MB, incl. fsync)
GC (100k objects) + cargo test -p ibg-cache <30 s; 100% pass

5.5 Conformance Fixtures

Fixture Purpose Expected output
fixtures/cache/hit_basic.json / miss_basic.json Pre-populated / empty cache cache_hits=1 no exec / cache_hits=0 exec called
fixtures/cache/cas_corrupt_object.json CAS object digest mismatch paranoid_verify=true → CasDigestMismatch; re-exec
fixtures/cache/gc_boundary.json Store at max_size_bytes, add 1 object GC evicts ≥1 old object
fixtures/cache/concurrent_insert.json 16 threads inserting same key Only one entry stored; no corruption
fixtures/cache/missing_cas_object.json Entry references digest absent from CAS Lookup returns None; exec proceeds

fixtures/cache/schema_migration.json Old cache format, new engine Safe fallback: treated as empty cache

5.6 Pitfalls

Pitfall Manifestation Root cause Detection Recovery
Stale hit on reused ActionKey Wrong output materialized Key omits tool version/env change Add tool digest + env hash to ActionKey (ibg-fingerprint) Widen key input set; GC old entries
CAS object missing at materialize get_object fails after lookup returned Some GC races the build Build-phase mutex excludes GC during active build Fallback re-exec on CasObjectNotFound
Corrupt tmp file / flock leak Disk leak or wrong-file rename / other processes block Crash between write+rename / no Drop guard Startup scan for stale *.tmp / RAII FileLock Cleanup routine; OS releases flock on fd close
GC deletes object mid-materialize Output file missing Concurrent GC + materialize, no refcounting GC must not delete referenced objects Refcount CAS objects during active builds
mmap index not flushed on crash Index diverges from CAS No msync before exit msync(MS_SYNC) at build end + startup CRC Rebuild index from CAS directory scan
ActionKey collision (theoretical) / large object OOM Wrong hit / OOM in get_object blake3 256-bit collision / no streaming read Log divergent inputs on suspicion Force re-exec; stream reads above streaming_threshold (100 MB)

5.7 Performance Concerns

  • mmap'd action_cache.bin gives O(1) lookup (~100 MB window at 1M entries, madvise(MADV_RANDOM)); CAS sharded 256-way avoids readdir on millions of flat-dir files; fsync (~0.5 ms NVMe, ~5 ms HDD) is an accepted correctness tax.
  • RwLock read-only lookups scale under parallelism with no contention; GC atime tracking can add I/O on cache-hot builds — use relatime or an explicit access log.
  • Materialize cost is N×(CAS read+write), target <500 ms for 100×1 MB outputs; batch CAS puts before the action-cache insert to cut fsync count; zstd level-3 compression (1 MB→~400 KB typical) is configurable via CacheConfig::compress.

5.8 Edge Cases

Edge case Handling
0 output files / symlink output CacheEntry{outputs:[]}, no-op materialize / target content stored, symlink recreated
IBG_CACHE_DIR missing / read-only Created on first use / CacheNotWritable, build proceeds uncached
Two actions share an output path Rejected at ibg-graph validation; never reaches cache
Cache dir on NFS (no flock) Detected, falls back to no multi-process locking, WARN cache_no_flock
max_size_bytes=0 / disk full on CAS write GC evicts everything every build (caching effectively off) / DiskFull, build continues uncached

5.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Detection Blast radius Recovery Operator visibility
CAS corruption (bit rot) Paranoid-mode digest mismatch Single action's output Delete corrupt object; re-exec ERROR cas_digest_mismatch
Action cache index corrupt CRC mismatch on startup All entries unusable ibg-cli cache repair (rebuild from CAS scan) WARN cache_index_corrupt; rebuilding
GC deletes live object Materialize fails mid-build Single target re-exec Refcount guard prevents this in steady state ERROR cas_object_missing_during_materialize
Disk full during CAS write Err(DiskFull) New results not cached Free disk; next build re-caches WARN cache_disk_full bytes_needed={n}

5.10 Operational Notes

  • Metrics: cache_hits/misses, cache_hit_rate, cas_objects_total, cas_bytes_total, gc_bytes_freed/objects_removed, cache_lookup_latency_us, cas_put_latency_us; tracing events CacheHit/CacheMiss/CasPut/CasGet/GcComplete.
  • CLI: ibg-cli cache stats|gc|repair|clear; schema version byte in action_cache.bin header, mismatch treated as empty cache (INFO cache_schema_upgraded).
  • Multi-process safety: flock(LOCK_EX) on insert, LOCK_SH on lookup, GC takes LOCK_EX; IBG_CACHE_COMPRESS=0 disables zstd; IBG_CACHE_PARANOID=1 verifies every read (5–15% throughput cost, recommended in CI).

18. Phase 6 — Action executor — spawn commands, capture declared outputs, working-dir mgmt, exit-code/stderr handling, environment control, output materialization from CAS

6.1 Objectives

Goal Constraint
Spawn action commands in a controlled, content-isolated working directory ($IBGROOT/exec/<action-key-prefix>/) Cancellable via CancellationToken (tokio-util); partial output dirs cleaned on cancel
Capture all declared outputs by Digest post-exec Only declared paths ingested; undeclared paths trigger a sandbox violation (Phase 10)
Normalize environment: hermetic env map from Action.env + allowlist No ambient $HOME/$PATH leakage unless explicitly declared
Handle exit codes: non-zero → Failed, ActionFailed stderr captured to a bounded 64 KB ring buffer
Materialize outputs from CAS on cache hit (skip exec) Hard-links on same FS; copy fallback cross-device
Executor trait consumed by ibg-sched Trait-object dispatch, async fn via async_trait

Scope: ibg-exec crate only; ibg-sandbox hooks plug in as a trait impl, not implemented until Phase 10. Working-dir create/cleanup must be atomic from the scheduler's perspective — slot acquired before mkdir, released after cleanup.

6.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

Modules: executor.rs (Executor trait + LocalExecutor), working_dir.rs (lifecycle), env.rs (EnvBuilder), capture.rs (OutputCapture), materialize.rs (CasMaterializer), stdio.rs (StdioCapture), error.rs (ExecError→IbgError).

Executor trait: async fn run(&self, action:&Action, ctx:ExecContext)->Result<ExecOutcome,ExecError> and async fn materialize(&self, entry:&CacheEntry, dst:&WorkingDir)->Result<(),ExecError>; ExecContext carries a CancellationToken, telemetry sender, CAS handle, and job-slot guard.

WorkingDir lifecycle: create(base,action_key) mkdirs base/<action_key_hex[..16]>/ with O_CLOEXEC; on success the scheduler calls commit_outputs(), on failure/cancel cleanup() removes the tree; output staging renames atomically into the final CAS ingest path.

EnvBuilder assembles a hermetic map: start empty, insert explicit Action.env entries, insert allowlisted passthrough (TMPDIR,USER) only if enabled, set IBG_ACTION_KEY/IBG_WORKING_DIR, and reject any key containing = or NUL as MalformedEnv.

OutputCapture: post-exit, iterates Action.outputs (paths relative to working dir), stat()s each (missing required output → MissingDeclaredOutput), computes a streaming blake3 Digest via ibg-fingerprint::digest_file() (ADR-04), and returns Vec<(PathBuf,Digest)> for the CAS store.

StdioCapture: tokio::process::Command with piped stdio; one async task drains stdout (discarded by default), another drains stderr into a wrap-around 64 KB ring buffer, emitting StderrTruncated on overflow.

CAS materialization (cache-hit path): for each CacheEntry.outputs[i], ibg-cache::cas_get(digest) then hard-link the blob into working_dir/<declared_path> (same-st_dev check), falling back to std::fs::copy cross-device; parent dirs created via create_dir_all before linking.

Observability: span!(action_exec,action_key,node_id) wraps run(); emits ExecStarted{node_id,action_key,ts}, ExecFinished{node_id,exit_code,duration_ms,output_digests}, and CasMaterialize{node_id,digest_count,duration_ms} on the cache-hit path.

Tests: MockExecutor (preset ExecOutcome, no real spawn) for unit tests; integration test spawns real echo/cat and verifies output digests against precomputed blake3.

6.3 Deliverable

  • ibg-exec compiles as a library, LocalExecutor runnable against the real filesystem; ibg-cli dispatches a single action end-to-end (resolve → cache miss → exec → CAS store).
  • Second ibg-cli build --target X invocation materializes from CAS with no subprocess spawn; ibg-bench::exec_throughput ≥500 actions/s on 8 cores; working-dir cleanup verified leak-free after cancellation.

6.4 Acceptance Criteria

Check Pass Condition
cargo test -p ibg-exec All unit + integration tests green
Missing output / non-zero exit MissingDeclaredOutput no CAS write / ActionFailed{exit_code} with stderr captured
Env hermetic + cache-hit materialize Spawned process can't observe undeclared HOME/PATH; hard-link increases CAS blob st_nlink
Cancel mid-exec Working dir removed, Cancelled, no partial CAS entry
Stderr truncation + exec throughput StderrTruncated at 64 KB, no panic; ≥500 actions/s (8-core, ibg-bench)
Output digest correctness blake3 of materialized file == stored CacheEntry digest

6.5 Conformance Fixtures

Fixture Purpose Expected Output
exec/happy_path.toml Action writes one declared file Digest matches precomputed blake3
exec/missing_output.toml / nonzero_exit.toml Output not created / exits 1 MissingDeclaredOutput / ActionFailed{exit_code:1}
exec/env_isolation.toml Action prints env stdout lacks HOME=
exec/cancel.toml Cancel after 50ms during a long sleep Cancelled; working dir absent
exec/cas_materialize.toml Pre-populated CAS, materialize File content matches CAS blob
exec/stderr_overflow.toml Command emits >64 KB stderr StderrTruncated; no OOM
exec/multi_output.toml Action declares 5 outputs All 5 digests stored in CAS

Malformed: NUL byte in env key → MalformedEnv. Replay: re-run twice, second is a cache hit (Phase 5 integration).

6.6 Pitfalls

Pitfall Manifestation Root Cause Recovery
TOCTOU on output capture Digest taken before fsync Buffered libc I/O, process exits before flush fsync declared output (or O_SYNC) before digesting
Working-dir collision Two actions share a dir Action-key prefix truncated too short Use ≥16 hex chars or a UUID suffix
Env passthrough creep Ambient var affects output Allowlist inadvertently includes LANG/LC_ALL Enumerate allowlist explicitly; sandbox rejects undeclared vars
Hard-link across devices EXDEV st_dev mismatch not checked pre-link Detect mismatch; copy fallback
stderr ring-buffer race / zombie processes Garbled logs / leaked child Shared buffer (should be per-action) / wait() skipped on cancel Per-action StdioCapture instance; drop impl calls kill()+wait()
Cancel races working-dir cleanup Stale CAS blobs CAS write and cancel run concurrently CAS write only after exit+capture; cancel token checked first

6.7 Performance Concerns

  • Blake3 per output is I/O-bound (mmap via memmap2 for files >8 MB); create_dir_all overhead amortized by pre-creating the base dir (per-action subdir is one mkdir); hard-link contention mitigated by 256-way CAS sharding.
  • Stderr drain tasks pooled via a bounded tokio::spawn budget rather than one per action; working-dir cleanup (rmdir -rf) runs in spawn_blocking to avoid blocking the async runtime.
  • Process spawn (fork+exec) costs ~1–5 ms cold with no mitigation beyond avoiding shell wrapping; cross-device CAS materialization copy is logged and best avoided by co-locating CAS + working dir on one filesystem.

6.8 Edge Cases

  • Zero declared outputs: valid side-effect-only action, empty capture vec, CAS write skipped; directory output: walked and stored as a Merkle subtree (forward-compat with ibg-remote).
  • Symlink as declared output: policy follows the symlink and stores target content, the link itself is not stored (documented limitation); working dir path > PATH_MAX: base length capped at 200 chars at config validation.
  • Concurrent cancel + natural exit: an AtomicBool completed flag prevents double-cleanup; command not found: NotFound on spawn → CommandNotFound, not retried; OOM-killed child: treated as ActionFailed{exit_code:-9}, stderr may be empty.

6.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Detection Blast Radius Recovery Operator Signal
Command not found spawn()→NotFound Single action Failed Fix command; rebuild CommandNotFound in build output
