Deadlock in work-stealing queue Dependency cycle not caught by graph layer Watchdog: zero-progress timeout (30 s) Abort build; log diagnostic Identify cycle via ibg-graph cycle-check tool
Job slot starvation All slots held by fan-in waiting on one slow dep Critical-path scheduling orders blocker first Scheduler detects convoy; deprioritizes waiters Increase --jobs; investigate slow action
Action spawn fails (ENOMEM, EMFILE) OS resource limits exec.spawn_error_total counter Retry after 1 s (1 attempt) Raise ulimit -n; reduce --jobs
Output file not created by action Action bug; action exit-0 without writing output Post-exec declared-output check Mark Failed; do not cache Fix action command
Sandbox seccomp policy too strict False-positive syscall block SandboxViolation on legitimate syscall None (policy is policy) Whitelist syscall in sandbox config

F6 — Persistence / State DB Failures

Symptom Root Cause Detection Auto Recovery Manual Recovery
DB corrupt on open Partial write / crash mid-update CRC mismatch, epoch tag invalid Truncate to last good epoch; mark all nodes Dirty Force --cold-build; delete DB
Schema version mismatch Binary upgrade without migration Version check at open Run migration shim Run ibg-cli db migrate
DB size growth unbounded Node tombstones not GC'd persist.db_size_bytes metric Periodic compaction (on build start if >threshold) ibg-cli db compact
Lost revisions after clock skew System clock jumped backward Revision monotonicity check Alert and rebuild affected nodes NTP sync; --reset-revision

F7 — File-Watch Failures

Symptom Root Cause Detection Auto Recovery Manual Recovery
Missed file change (silent under-build) inotify queue overflow watch.events_dropped_total counter Polling fallback re-scans on overflow Increase inotify.max_queued_events; reduce watch breadth
Watch process leak ibg-watch crash not propagated Parent receives SIGCHLD Restart watcher; trigger full re-scan Kill zombie; ibg-cli watch restart
Spurious watch events (false-positive rebuild) FS metadata-only updates (atime) Count engine.nodes_rebuilt vs changed Suppress atime-only events (already filtered) Mount with noatime; configure FS

F8 — Remote Execution Failures

Symptom Root Cause Detection Auto Recovery Manual Recovery
Remote exec returns wrong digest RE backend bug or MITM Digest verification at fetch Re-exec locally; report to RE operator Disable remote exec; use local only
Merkle tree upload hangs Network stall / large input Per-upload timeout Cancel; retry locally Increase timeout; check network
Remote cache push fails silently Backend quota / auth remote.push_error_total Log warning; continue (push is best-effort) Fix auth/quota; ops alert

F9 — Memory / Resource Failures

Symptom Root Cause Detection Auto Recovery Manual Recovery
OOM kill of ibg-engine In-process memo store + large graph unconstrained build.oom_kills_total; OS SIGKILL Restart; persist DB intact; incremental resume Reduce --memo-cache-mb; reduce --jobs
File descriptor leak Watch handles + CAS fds not closed exec.fd_count gauge; EMFILE on spawn Watchdog closes idle CAS fds lsof diagnosis; upgrade binary
Thread panic in worker Unhandled Err in task; expect in scheduler std::panic::catch_unwind in worker loop; counter Mark associated node Failed; continue other nodes Check log for panic backtrace; file bug
Disk I/O timeout (slow NFS) NFS mount stall during fingerprint or CAS write Timeout per FS op (10 s default); io.timeout_total Retry once; then fail node Unmount NFS; use local FS for workspace

Recovery Decision Tree

Show the original text diagram
Build fails
  ├─ Correctness suspected (under-build)?
  │    → --cold-build; capture DB snapshot; investigate sandbox logs
  ├─ DB corrupt?
  │    → ibg-cli db check → truncate/discard → cold build
  ├─ CAS disk full?
  │    → ibg-cli cas gc --force → resume build
  ├─ Remote cache down?
  │    → Automatic fallback to local exec; no operator action needed
  └─ Action timeout / hang?
       → ibg-cli kill-action <node_id> → re-queue

Forensic Artifacts

  • Structured log: invocation_id, node_id, action_key, failure_kind, exit_code, stderr_digest.
  • ibg-telemetry trace spans: parent invocation → node eval → exec → cache ops.
  • Persist DB epoch log: last-good epoch timestamp.
  • Sandbox violation dump: syscall, path, action_key.
  • Action replay bundle: ibg-cli export-action <action_key> → tarball of inputs + command; enables offline reproduction.
  • Critical-path log: ordered list of node_ids on critical path with duration per node; attached to every invocation trace.

Operational Readiness

Metrics Strategy (ibg-telemetry)

Metric Type Labels Purpose
build.duration_seconds Histogram invocation_id End-to-end build latency distribution
build.nodes_total Gauge kind (source/action/glob) Graph size per invocation
build.rebuilt_total Counter reason (cache_miss/input_changed/forced) Rebuild volume
build.cached_total Counter source (local/remote) Cache effectiveness
build.skipped_total Counter — Early-cutoff effectiveness
cache.hit_rate Gauge — ActionKey hit / (hit+miss); primary SLO metric
cas.disk_used_bytes Gauge — Disk pressure
cas.evictions_total Counter — GC activity
sched.ready_queue_depth Gauge — Scheduler pressure
sched.critical_path_seconds Histogram — Lower-bound build time
exec.duration_seconds Histogram action_kind Per-action execution latency
exec.spawn_error_total Counter error_kind OS resource exhaustion
fingerprint.hash_duration_p99 Gauge — Hashing bottleneck
persist.db_size_bytes Gauge — State DB growth
watch.events_dropped_total Counter — inotify overflow indicator
security.sandbox_violations_total Counter violation_kind Hermeticity failures
remote.push_error_total Counter backend Remote cache health
remote.fetch_latency_seconds Histogram backend Remote cache latency

Tracing Strategy

  • OpenTelemetry (OTLP) traces from ibg-telemetry; Jaeger / Tempo compatible.
  • Trace hierarchy: build_invocation → node_eval[node_id] → cache_lookup | exec[action_key] → sandbox + cas_store.
  • Attributes: invocation_id (UUID), node_id, action_key (hex truncated to 16 chars for display), cache_source, exit_code.
  • Sampling: 100 % for failures; configurable rate for success (default 10 %).
  • Critical-path trace: tag the chain of nodes on the critical path with on_critical_path=true.

Structured Logging

  • Format: JSON (serde_json); fields: ts (RFC3339), level, invocation_id, node_id, action_key, message, error.
  • Levels: ERROR — correctness failures, sandbox violations, CRC mismatches; WARN — degraded mode (remote unavailable, GC triggered); INFO — build start/end, cache-hit summary; DEBUG — per-node state transitions; TRACE — fingerprint detail.
  • Log rotation: operator-configured via env IBG_LOG_FILE + IBG_LOG_MAX_BYTES; defaults stderr only.

Dashboard Design

┌─ Build Overview ──────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  Cache Hit Rate   [sparkline]    SLO: ≥80%               │
│  Build Duration   [histogram]    P95 target               │
│  Rebuilt / Skipped / Cached    [stacked bar]              │
├─ Scheduler ───────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│  Ready Queue Depth [gauge]  Critical Path [histogram]     │
│  Slot Utilization  [heatmap by time]                      │
├─ CAS / Persist ───────────────────────────────────────────┤
│  Disk Used [gauge vs quota]  Evictions [rate]             │
│  DB Size [gauge]             Compaction Events [counter]  │
├─ Health / Errors ─────────────────────────────────────────┤
│  Sandbox Violations [rate]   Spawn Errors [rate]          │
│  Remote Push Errors [rate]   Watch Drops [rate]           │
└───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

Alerting Philosophy

  • Alert on user-visible correctness and availability; not on internal metrics unless they predict an outage.
  • PagerDuty-class (immediate): sandbox violation rate > 0 sustained 5 min; CRC mismatch on persist open; CAS disk > 95 %.
  • Warning (ticket): cache hit rate < 70 % for 30 min; watch events dropped > 100/s; remote push error rate > 5 %.
  • All alerts include invocation_id, link to trace, runbook URL.

SLO Definitions

SLO Target Measurement Window
Cache hit rate (local) ≥ 80 % of action evaluations Rolling 1 h
Incremental build correctness 0 under-builds (stale outputs served) Per invocation
Build availability (no crash) 99.5 % of invocations complete without panic Rolling 24 h
Persist recovery time < 10 s from crash to next build start Measured at restart
Remote cache fetch P95 < 2 s per blob Rolling 1 h

Incident Response

  1. Triage via dashboard: identify metric spike (correctness vs performance vs availability).
  2. Correctness incident (under-build suspected): immediately disable incremental mode (--cold-build); capture persist DB snapshot for forensics; trigger full rebuild.
  3. Disk exhaustion: run ibg-cli cas gc --force; if insufficient, expand quota or purge oldest entries.
  4. Sandbox bypass confirmed: halt affected action class; notify security; patch sandbox policy; audit recent cache entries from that action.
  5. Rollback: revert binary; persist DB schema migrations are backward-compatible to N-1 (see Compatibility); old binary reads new DB safely.

Runbooks (summary, linked in alert bodies)

  • runbook/cold-build.md — when and how to force --cold-build; safety checks.
  • runbook/db-compact.md — ibg-cli db compact; expected duration; abort criteria.
  • runbook/cas-gc.md — GC watermarks; force-eviction; impact on ongoing builds.
  • runbook/sandbox-policy.md — add syscall whitelist; rebuild sandbox; test hermetic action set.
  • runbook/remote-cache-failover.md — disable remote; monitor local fallback; re-enable when backend stable.

Backup Strategy

  • ibg-persist DB: snapshot to object store on every Nth build (operator-configured N, default 100) using atomic rename of DB file copy.
  • CAS: content-addressed → inherently reconstructable by re-running builds; optional push to remote CAS for durability.
  • Restore test: monthly automated test restores DB snapshot to staging env; validates incremental build produces same outputs as cold build.
  • Backup retention: keep last 7 DB snapshots; prune older atomically.
  • Never backup in-progress build state: snapshot only after successful checkpoint.

Capacity Planning

Resource Formula Tuning knob
CAS disk avg_output_bytes × unique_action_count × replication_factor --cas-quota-gb
Persist DB ~1 KiB per node × node_count graph.max_nodes
In-process memo ~200 B per node × hot_set_size --memo-cache-mb
Worker threads --jobs ≤ logical CPU count; default = nproc --jobs
inotify watches 1 per source file; OS limit /proc/sys/fs/inotify/max_user_watches OS tunable

Performance SLOs (Non-Correctness)

SLO Target Notes
Null build (no changes) < 500 ms for 100k-node graph Engine + fingerprint fast-path
Incremental build (1-file change) < 2 s fan-out latency before first exec starts Dirty propagation + scheduler
Cache lookup + materialize < 100 ms P95 for local CAS Mmap-backed CAS index
Critical-path scheduling overhead < 5 % of critical-path wall time Priority queue update cost

Deployment Topology

Show the original text diagram
Developer workstation: ibg-cli → ibg-engine (in-process) → local CAS + persist DB
                                                          → optional remote cache (gRPC)
CI worker: same binary; remote cache shared; remote exec optional
Remote cache server: content-addressed blob store (S3-compatible or custom); no state beyond blobs
  • Stateless binary: all state in persist DB (local) + CAS (local or remote); binary can be replaced without drain.
  • Multi-user workspace: one persist DB per workspace root; concurrent ibg-cli invocations use file-lock on DB; second invocation waits or errors.

Rolling-Upgrade / Canary Strategy

  • Binary upgrade: new binary reads old DB (backward-compatible schema); runs migration shim on first open.
  • Canary: route 5 % of CI workers to new binary; compare build.cached_total / build.rebuilt_total ratios; promote if no correctness alerts in 24 h.
  • Rollback: kill new binary; restart old binary; old binary reads DB (forward-compat guaranteed for N-1); migration is reversible if annotated as such.

Compatibility & Versioning

API Stability (ibg-core / ibg-engine public types)

Surface Stability Class Policy
NodeId, Revision, Digest, ActionKey Stable Semver major required to change representation
BuildNode, Action, NodeState structs Stable (additive) New optional fields in minor; remove in major
IbgError enum variants Stable (additive) New variants in minor; #[non_exhaustive] enforced
BuildRequest / BuildResult Stable Same as structs above
Internal engine state machine Unstable Not part of public API; may change in minor
ibg-telemetry metric names Stable after 1.0 Rename only in major; add in minor

Wire Compatibility (Remote Cache Protocol)

  • Remote cache interface (ibg-remote) follows a subset of the Bazel Remote Execution API (REAPI v2).
  • Digest algorithm pinned: BLAKE3-256; encoded as hex string in JSON / bytes in protobuf.
  • Merkle tree format: stable; additive fields allowed in minor; structural changes require major + negotiation.
  • gRPC service version field in headers; client sends min + preferred version; server rejects unsupported.

Storage Compatibility (ibg-persist DB)

Version Change Strategy
Add new column / record type Minor version; old binary ignores unknown record types (tag-length-value format)
Rename / remove column Major version; migration shim rewrites DB before use
Change hash algorithm (hypothetical) Major version; full cold rebuild required; old DB discarded
Change Revision semantics Major version; DB replaced
  • Schema version stored in DB header as u32; checked on open.
  • Migration: ibg-persist::migrate(old_version, new_version, &mut db) — applied automatically on first open.
  • Backward compatibility: binary at version X reads DB written by X-1; forward compatibility (X-1 reads X DB): guaranteed for minor; not guaranteed for major.
  • Migration dry-run: ibg-cli db migrate --dry-run — reports changes without writing.

Semantic-Versioning Policy

  • 0.x.y: no compatibility guarantees (development phase).
  • 1.0.0: public API frozen per semver; patch = bug fix; minor = additive; major = breaking.
  • MSRV (1.78) bumps: treated as minor version increase; announced in CHANGELOG 60 days before.
  • Crate version alignment: all ibg-* crates share the same version number; published together.

Deprecation Policy

  • Deprecated items annotated #[deprecated(since = "X.Y", note = "...")].
  • Deprecated items remain for at least one full major version cycle (e.g., deprecated in 1.2 → removed no earlier than 2.0).
  • Deprecation notice in CHANGELOG + docs; migration guide provided.

Rolling-Upgrade Guarantees

  • N and N-1 binaries can coexist in a CI fleet sharing a remote cache: ActionKey format is stable across minor versions; cache entries are interoperable.
  • Local persist DB is single-writer; no mixed-version DB write scenario possible.
  • Telemetry metric names stable after 1.0; dashboards survive binary upgrades within major version.

Migration Guarantees

  • Cold-build fallback: any migration failure → persist DB discarded → cold build → correct result; correctness never sacrificed for migration convenience.
  • Migration idempotent: running migrate twice on same DB is a no-op.
  • Migration atomicity: new DB written to temp file; renamed atomically on success; old file retained as .bak for one build cycle.

Compatibility Matrix

ibg binary DB schema Remote cache Notes
1.x 1.x (auto-migrate) REAPI v2 Same major: guaranteed
2.x 1.x (with migration shim) REAPI v2 Major: shim required; announce 90 days ahead
1.x 2.x (newer schema) REAPI v2 Unsupported; fail fast with clear error
1.x CI + 1.x dev shared remote CAS REAPI v2 ActionKey stable across builds; interoperable

Cross-Platform Digest Consistency

  • Digest computed over file content bytes only (no platform-specific metadata); identical content → identical Digest on Linux/macOS/Windows.
  • Path separators normalized to / before hashing; no OS-native separator in ActionKey.
  • Env var casing: case-sensitive on all platforms for ActionKey purposes (even Windows); declared explicitly.
  • Executable bit: included in input metadata hash for source nodes; platform difference exposed if executable bit meaning differs.

Feature Flag / Experimental API Policy

  • Experimental features gated behind #[cfg(feature = "experimental")]; not included in stable releases.
  • Experimental metrics prefixed exp.; subject to removal without major version bump.
  • Feature flags documented in CHANGELOG with target-stable version or deprecation timeline.
  • No experimental features enabled by default in release builds; opt-in via IBG_FEATURES=experimental.

Long-Term Maintainability Commitments

  • All unsafe blocks audited and documented with safety invariant comments; tracked in SAFETY.md.
  • Fuzzing targets (cargo-fuzz) for: ActionKey serialization, persist DB decode, NodeKey validation, sandbox policy parser.
  • MSRV tested in CI on every commit; rust-toolchain.toml pins minimum version.
  • Changelogs follow Keep-a-Changelog format; machine-parseable for release automation.
  • API surface reviewed against semver-checks before every release; CI blocks on unannounced breaking changes.

Testing & Verification Strategy

Unit Tests — Per-Crate Isolation

Crate Key unit targets Invariants verified
ibg-core NodeId/Revision/Digest encoding, IbgError variants no truncation; u32 NodeId round-trips
ibg-graph DAG construction, cycle detection, topo-sort, rev-dep index topological order consistent; cycle detected O(V+E)
ibg-fingerprint blake3 digest correctness, stat fast-path, ActionKey derivation deterministic across platforms; sorted inputs
ibg-engine dirty/verified state machine, early-cutoff trigger, revision monotonicity no stale serve; no over-build on unchanged output digest
ibg-cache ActionKey lookup, CAS insert/read, eviction policy no spurious miss; no corrupt object served
ibg-exec command spawn, declared-output capture, env isolation missing declared output → IbgError::OutputNotProduced
ibg-sched work-stealing dequeue, critical-path ordering, backpressure deadlock-free under zero-slot saturation
ibg-watch event dedup, debounce window, dirty propagation no lost event within debounce; poll fallback parity
ibg-persist encode/decode round-trip, schema version header, corrupt-detect torn write falls back to full re-eval
ibg-sandbox undeclared-input detection, declared-output enforcement undeclared access → hard error, never silently ignored
ibg-remote Merkle input tree construction, REAPI serialization deterministic tree hash across orderings
ibg-telemetry counter/gauge/histogram precision, trace span nesting no dropped event under 1k spans/s
  • Use #[cfg(test)] + rstest for parametric table-driven cases.
  • Digest property: blake3(b) == Digest(blake3::hash(b).as_bytes()) — always assert.
  • Revision monotonicity: unit test — 1000 concurrent fetch_add; assert final == 1000.
  • Cycle detection: golden DAGs (linear, diamond, fan-in/fan-out, 2-node cycle, self-loop) — assert each outcome exactly.

Integration Tests — Cross-Crate Flows

  • tests/incremental_basic: source-change → engine re-eval → exec → cache store; assert BuildResult counts.
  • tests/early_cutoff: source change produces same action output digest → dependents stay verified; assert skipped count.
  • tests/dynamic_deps: action discovers additional input at runtime → edge added; next build includes it.
  • tests/cache_hit: repeat build with no changes → zero exec calls; cache-hit count == action count.
  • tests/cycle_reject: graph with dynamic dep forming cycle → IbgError::CycleDetected, no deadlock.
  • tests/crash_persist: write partial persist state, restart → full re-eval, correct result.
  • tests/file_watch_invalidation: write file, assert watch propagates dirty to dependents within 200 ms.
  • tests/sandbox_violation: action reads undeclared input → IbgError::UndeclaredInput; no cached result stored.
  • tests/remote_roundtrip: mock REAPI backend; action cache hit via remote; assert local store populated.
  • Integration tests use a temp-dir fixture; all paths are absolute; no global mutable state.

Differential / Comparative Testing

  • Reference oracle: a naive make-style full-rebuild driver for the same action graph.
  • For every incremental build scenario: assert incremental_result.outputs == oracle_result.outputs (byte-identical).
  • Fuzz-driven differential: random edit sequences applied to both engines; divergence = bug.
  • Compare against Shake semantics on equivalent graphs (dynamic-dep discovery, early cutoff) via golden traces.

Property-Based Testing (proptest / quickcheck)

Property Generator Assertion
Graph round-trip arbitrary DAG (no cycle) encode → decode → same adj list
ActionKey stability permute input list sorted before hash → same key
Early-cutoff idempotency arbitrary chain verify(verify(G)) == verify(G)
Revision monotonicity concurrent bump sequence final == N bumps
Digest collision-resistance random 64-byte pairs Digest(a) != Digest(b) w.h.p.
Cache soundness arbitrary ActionKey/result pairs lookup(insert(k,v),k) == Some(v)
  • Shrinking: proptest shrinks to minimal failing graph size for diagnosis.

Fuzzing (cargo-fuzz / libfuzzer)

  • fuzz_targets/decode_persist: arbitrary bytes → ibg-persist decoder; assert no panic/UB.
  • fuzz_targets/graph_construction: arbitrary node/edge sequences; assert no panic; cycle detection robust.
  • fuzz_targets/fingerprint_input: arbitrary file content + stat; assert no crash.
  • fuzz_targets/cache_key: arbitrary ActionKey bytes; no panic on lookup.
  • Run in CI on every PR for 60 s; nightly for 4 h; corpus committed under fuzz/corpus/.

Chaos, Fault Injection, Soak

  • Fault injection: randomly return Err(IbgError::Io(...)) from persist/exec/cache writes; assert recovery.
  • Scheduler chaos: randomly delay work-stealing dequeue (10–100 ms); assert eventual completion, no deadlock.
  • Watch chaos: drop/duplicate fs events; assert eventual consistency (no missed dirty after 2× debounce window).
  • Soak test: 10k-node graph, 1000 random incremental edits over 30 min; assert zero incorrect outputs.
  • OOM injection: cap allocator at threshold; assert graceful IbgError::ResourceExhausted, no silent wrong output.

Deterministic Replay

  • ibg-telemetry emits structured trace log (action inputs, outputs, revisions, cache decisions).
  • Replay harness: feed logged build session back through engine in deterministic mode; assert same decisions.
  • Catches non-determinism in action output or scheduling.

Performance Regression Testing

  • Benchmark suite (ibg-bench) gates CI: wall-clock, peak RSS, cache-hit rate.
  • Regressions > 5% on p50 latency or > 10% on peak RSS → CI failure.
  • Tracked benchmarks: null-build (all cached), single-source-change (incremental), cold-build (no cache).
  • Historical results committed as JSON baselines; cargo bench --baseline enforced in CI.

Conformance and Compatibility

  • REAPI conformance: test against Bazel Remote Execution reference server (nativelink or bazel-buildfarm).
  • Schema version compatibility: tests load persist DB written by N-1 minor version; assert clean upgrade path.
  • Platform matrix: macOS (arm64, x86-64), Linux (x86-64, arm64), via CI matrix.

Build & Release Engineering

Reproducible Builds

  • Lockfile: Cargo.lock committed; no wildcard version specs in workspace Cargo.toml.
  • Build environment pinned: Rust toolchain via rust-toolchain.toml (MSRV 1.78 + nightly for fuzz/bench).
  • SOURCE_DATE_EPOCH propagated; build timestamps clamped for reproducibility.
  • cargo auditable embeds dependency tree in binary for post-build SBOM extraction.
  • Binary diff: two independent CI builds of same commit produce byte-identical artifacts (verified by SHA256 comparison job).
  • RUSTFLAGS=-C target-cpu=generic for release artifacts unless platform-specific builds are explicitly published.

Artifact Signing and Provenance

Artifact Signing mechanism Verification
Release binaries cosign keyless (Sigstore OIDC) cosign verify --certificate-identity
Container images cosign + SBOM attestation Rekor transparency log
Crate publishes cargo publish via CI service account crates.io token scoped to publish
SBOM (CycloneDX JSON) bundled + uploaded to release assets verifiable via cosign attestation
  • SLSA Build Level 2: CI provenance attestation generated per release (GitHub Actions OIDC → SLSA provenance JSON).
  • Binary checksum file (SHA256SUMS) signed with same cosign identity; published alongside binaries.

SBOM Generation

  • cargo cyclonedx generates CycloneDX JSON SBOM from workspace at each release.
  • SBOM uploaded as GitHub release asset and attached as cosign attestation to container image.
  • License compliance scan: cargo deny check licenses — deny list includes GPL-incompatible; CI gate.

Dependency Auditing and Supply-Chain

  • cargo audit runs on every PR; CI blocks on any RUSTSEC advisory.
  • cargo deny check enforces: no duplicate versions of critical crates (blake3, tokio, serde); no banned licenses.
  • Dependabot / Renovate configured for automated patch PRs; auto-merge on patch + green CI.
  • cargo vet (Mozilla): vetting policies for all direct deps; trusted publishers list maintained.
  • Pinned GitHub Actions SHA (no mutable tags); third-party actions limited to allowlist.

CI/CD Topology

Show the original text diagram
PR push:
  lint (clippy --deny warnings, rustfmt)
  → test matrix (stable + MSRV 1.78, linux x86-64 + arm64, macOS arm64)
  → cargo audit + cargo deny
  → fuzz (60 s, libfuzzer)
  → bench regression gate (vs. committed baseline)
  → integration tests (temp-dir sandbox)

Nightly:
  → fuzz (4 h, all targets)
  → soak test (10k-node, 1000 edits, 30 min)
  → SBOM refresh

Release (tag vX.Y.Z):
  → reproducible build (two independent runners, SHA256 diff)
  → SLSA provenance generation
  → cosign sign + SBOM attestation
  → crate publish (ibg-core first, dependents in dep order)
  → GitHub release + SHA256SUMS upload

Version Stamping

  • Version embedded at compile time: BUILD_GIT_SHA, BUILD_DATE, CARGO_PKG_VERSION via build.rs.
  • ibg-cli --version emits: ibg 0.1.0 (abc1234 2026-06-04).
  • Workspace crates versioned in lockstep; single version = "X.Y.Z" in root Cargo.toml propagated via workspace.package.

Release Channels

Channel Branch Cadence Consumers
stable main tagged monthly production integrators
beta release/X.Y bi-weekly early adopters
nightly main HEAD daily CI dogfooding
  • SemVer: MAJOR = breaking graph/cache API; MINOR = new crate or feature; PATCH = bug/perf fix.
  • ibg-core ABI stability: NodeId/Digest/Revision wire format frozen within MAJOR; persist schema versioned independently.

Release Automation

  • Release PR: bot bumps versions, updates CHANGELOG (from conventional commits), opens PR.
  • Merge → tag → CI release pipeline triggered automatically.
  • cargo publish order enforced: ibg-core → ibg-graph/ibg-fingerprint → ibg-engine/ibg-cache/ibg-exec → ibg-sched/ibg-watch/ibg-persist/ibg-sandbox/ibg-remote/ibg-telemetry → ibg-cli.
  • Rollback: crates.io yanks applied if critical correctness bug; GitHub release marked pre-release; incident playbook in docs/runbooks/release-rollback.md.

Performance Engineering

Benchmarking Strategy

M# Benchmark Fixture Metric Target
M1 null-build 10k-node all-cached wall-clock, RSS < 500 ms, < 150 MB
M2 cold-build 10k-node, no cache wall-clock per node < 2 ms amortized
M3 single-source-edit 1 leaf changed in 10k DAG incremental latency < 100 ms
M4 early-cutoff chain 10-deep chain, leaf changed, same output nodes re-executed 0 (all cutoff)
M5 cache-lookup throughput 100k ActionKey lookups ops/s > 500k/s
M6 parallel action exec 1000 independent actions, 8 cores speedup > 6× vs serial
M7 persist roundtrip 50k node state DB write + read time < 200 ms
M8 watch latency single file write → dirty propagation end-to-end < 50 ms p99
  • ibg-bench uses criterion for statistical rigor (warm-up, outlier rejection, confidence intervals).
  • Baselines committed as benches/baseline/*.json; CI --baseline comparison enforced.
  • Micro-benchmarks: blake3 throughput (target: saturate memory bandwidth), topo-sort (target: < 1 ms per 10k nodes), work-stealing dequeue under contention.

Profiling Methodology

  • CPU profiling: perf record -g (Linux) / Instruments (macOS); cargo flamegraph wrapper.
  • Allocation profiling: heaptrack or dhat; track peak live bytes + allocation count on hot paths.
  • Lock contention: perf lock / lltng-ust mutex wait; target < 1% wall-clock in lock.
  • Syscall analysis: strace -c / dtrace; minimize open/stat/read on hot incremental path (stat fast-path: single fstat, no re-read if mtime+size match).
  • Cache-line analysis: valgrind --tool=cachegrind; ensure NodeState fits cache line (64 B); pad if needed.

Flamegraph Workflow

Show the original text diagram
1. cargo build --release --features bench-instrumentation
2. cargo flamegraph --bin ibg-cli -- build --target //... (large fixture)
3. Open flamegraph.svg; identify hot frames
4. Annotate hot functions with #[inline(never)] if collapsing attribution
5. Iterate: isolate → measure → patch → re-measure
  • Common hot-path targets: blake3::hash (expected ~40% CPU on cold build), ibg-engine::eval_node (incremental path), ibg-sched::steal (under saturation).
  • Differential flamegraphs: before/after patch to confirm regression/improvement.

Tracing Methodology

  • ibg-telemetry emits OpenTelemetry spans: build, eval_node, cache_lookup, exec_action, persist_flush.
  • Trace backend: OTLP → Jaeger (local dev) / vendor OTLP endpoint (CI).
  • Span attributes: NodeId, ActionKey (hex-truncated), Revision, cache_hit (bool), exec_duration_ms.
  • Critical-path trace: annotate scheduler's critical path; confirm wall-clock dominated by critical-path depth × avg action latency.
  • Tail-latency: p99 eval_node span < 5 ms for cache hits; p99 exec_action span budget = action runtime + < 10 ms overhead.

Allocator and Memory Analysis

  • Default allocator: system; switch to tikv-jemallocator if RSS regression observed (< 10% overhead target).
  • Arena allocation for graph construction phase: pre-allocate NodeId slab; avoid per-node heap alloc.
  • Arc vs. owned: BuildNode owned in graph; Digest cheaply copied (32 B); no Arc<String> on hot path.
  • Peak RSS target for 100k-node graph: < 1 GB.
  • Memory leak check: valgrind --leak-check=full on integration test suite in CI (Linux only).

Throughput and Latency Targets (Summary)

M# Path p50 p99 Notes
M9 Cache hit (local) < 1 ms < 5 ms includes fingerprint verify
M10 Cache miss → exec → store action runtime + < 20 ms action runtime + < 50 ms overhead only
M11 Dirty propagation (watch) < 20 ms < 50 ms debounce + reverse-edge traversal
M12 Persist flush (50k nodes) < 100 ms < 200 ms async, non-blocking to eval
M13 Scheduler steal < 10 µs < 100 µs per steal attempt
  • Tail-latency root cause analysis: track p99.9 outliers in telemetry; correlate with persist flush or GC events.

Continuous Performance Tracking

  • ibg-bench results uploaded to Bencher.dev or internal time-series store after each main-branch merge.
  • Regression alert: > 5% wall-clock increase OR > 10% RSS increase triggers Slack alert + CI block on next PR.
  • Perf budget per phase: engine eval overhead ≤ 5% of total build time on 10k-node graph.

Scalability Analysis

Graph Scale Ceilings

Dimension Practical ceiling Bottleneck Mitigation
Node count ~10M nodes NodeId u32 (4.29B hard limit); topo-sort RAM 64-bit NodeId upgrade path; streaming topo
Edge count ~50M edges reverse-dep index memory compressed adjacency (delta-encoded)
Concurrent actions ~10k parallel scheduler channel + OS fd limits batched ready-queue; fd pool
Watch paths ~500k inotify watch limit (default 8k) bulk watch via directory-level + filter
Persist DB size ~10 GB mmap size on 32-bit; write amplification 64-bit mmap; LSM compaction backend
ActionKey space 2^256 none (blake3) —

Vertical Scaling

  • CPU: work-stealing saturates available cores; critical-path depth sets wall-clock floor regardless of cores.
  • RAM: peak RSS linear in node count × NodeState size (< 128 B/node target); 100k nodes < 128 MB metadata.
  • Disk I/O: CAS writes proportional to changed-output bytes; SSDs required for < 20 ms persist flush.
  • Thread count: ibg-sched spawns min(N_cores, job_slots) tokio tasks; configurable via --jobs.

Horizontal Scaling (Remote Cache / Execution)

  • ibg-remote shards action cache by leading ActionKey bytes (consistent hashing on remote cluster).
  • No state shared between local evaluators except via remote cache + CAS; horizontal scale-out is embarrassingly parallel per target set.
  • Remote execution (REAPI): multiple remote workers; scheduler dispatches to least-loaded; no global lock.
  • Cache replication: CAS objects are immutable (content-addressed); replication is safe and idempotent.
  • Remote cache invalidation: never needed — ActionKey encodes all inputs; a changed input produces a new key.

Scheduler Saturation

  • Saturation point: when all job slots busy AND ready-queue depth < worker count → workers spin-steal.
  • Back-pressure: ibg-sched applies credit-based back-pressure; graph evaluator blocks new node dispatch when ready-queue > 10× job_slots.
  • Critical-path ordering: nodes on critical path (longest remaining chain) get priority steal; reduces wall-clock under saturation.
  • Pathological case: diamond fan-in with single long action at root → all workers idle waiting; detected via telemetry critical-path trace.
  • Mitigation: expose --critical-path flag to surface longest path; users can split long actions.

Storage Growth and GC

  • CAS growth: proportional to unique output bytes across all builds; deduplication by Digest.
  • GC strategy: mark-sweep per ibg-cache; retain all objects reachable from any persisted NodeState; evict LRU beyond --cache-size-limit.
  • GC triggers: size threshold (configurable, default 10 GB) OR time-based (weekly); incremental GC to avoid > 1 s pause.
  • Persist DB compaction: if LSM-backed (e.g., rocksdb via rust-rocksdb), compaction scheduled during idle.
  • Metadata scaling: NodeState DB grows with graph; prune states for nodes no longer in graph on each build.

Network Fanout (Remote)

  • Remote cache upload: parallel uploads bounded by --upload-concurrency (default 32); back-pressure via semaphore.
  • Merkle input tree: O(input files) hashes uploaded; large trees (> 10k files) uploaded in parallel batches.
  • Bandwidth ceiling: gigabit LAN ~125 MB/s upload; 10k × 1 MB outputs → ~80 s; mitigated by content-dedup (only new digests uploaded).
  • Remote RPC timeout: all remote calls have per-call deadline (default 30 s) + retry with exponential backoff (3 attempts); fallback to local exec on remote failure.

Coordination Overhead

  • No global coordinator: demand-driven eval is per-target, pull-based; no central task dispatcher.
  • Revision counter: single global AtomicU64; contention negligible at < 10M increments/build.
  • Persist flush: single writer (append-only log or WAL); readers MVCC via snapshot; no reader/writer lock on hot eval path.
  • Dynamic dep registration: per-node mutex in ibg-graph; contention limited to nodes with many concurrent dynamic-dep discoverers (rare).

Replication and Consistency

  • Remote CAS: eventually consistent reads acceptable (content-addressed; any replica with correct Digest is valid).
  • Action cache: strong consistency required for correctness; recommend single primary with read replicas + write-through.
  • Local persist: strictly consistent (single writer process); multi-process access not supported (file lock enforced).
  • Distributed locking: not used; ActionKey uniqueness eliminates need for distributed consensus on cache writes.

Scaling Failure Modes

Failure Symptom Detection Recovery
inotify watch exhaustion watch silently drops events ibg-watch error log; health check polling fallback auto-engaged
Scheduler deadlock (cycle) all workers blocked cycle detector; telemetry idle-worker alert IbgError::CycleDetected; rebuild aborted
Persist DB corruption crash + torn write CRC check on load full re-eval; DB rebuilt
CAS disk full exec fails to store output IbgError::StorageFull evict LRU; alert operator
Remote timeout storm all remote calls timeout telemetry remote_error_rate > 5% fallback to local exec; alert
NodeId u32 overflow build fails at 4.29B nodes static assert + runtime check upgrade to u64 (major version)
Ready-queue saturation workers spin, latency spikes scheduler backpressure metric > threshold reduce job_slots; tune concurrency

Mental Models & First Principles

The compressed insights an expert in build systems holds. Each predicts a bug class. Every cited § resolves to a real heading.

MM1. The action key is a pure function of inputs; the cache is just a memo table. key = hash(command, input digests, env, tool digest); identical key ⇒ identical output, fetchable instead of executed. Predicts: the ≥ 95% warm-cache hit rate. Bug without it: omitting any real input (env var, tool version) from the key → a stale cache hit serving wrong outputs. Applied: §8 (ActionKey), Phase 6–7, §4.3 (cache hit rate), I-correctness.

MM2. Correctness depends on fingerprinting content, not timestamps. mtime is a fast-path hint; the source of truth is the content digest. Predicts: correct rebuilds after touch-without-change or clock skew. Bug without it: trusting mtime alone rebuilds unchanged files (waste) or skips changed-but-same-mtime files (corruption). Applied: §8 (fingerprint), Phase 5, §4.1 (incremental eval / fingerprint throughput).

MM3. Early cutoff is the real speedup: stop when a derived output is unchanged. A node going green means its dependents need not re-run even if an upstream input changed but produced the same output. Predicts: why a comment-only edit rebuilds almost nothing. Bug without it: propagating "dirty" transitively without checking output equality → rebuilding the world for a no-op change. Applied: Phase 6 (early cutoff), §8 (NodeState green/red), §4.3.

MM4. Hermeticity is the precondition for caching: an action must declare all its inputs. An undeclared input (a system header, an env var) makes the cache unsound. Predicts: why sandboxing detects undeclared reads. Bug without it: a cached action that secretly depended on /usr/include serves wrong results on a different machine. Applied: Phase 8 (sandbox), §4.6 (undeclared input detected), MM1.

MM5. The build is a DAG; cycles are a user error to detect, not to tolerate. Topological order is the only valid execution order; a cycle has none. Predicts: the cycle-detection requirement and critical-path scheduling. Bug without it: a cycle causes deadlock or infinite re-eval instead of a clear error. Applied: §8 (graph model), Phase 2–4, §4.1 (graph construction).

MM6. The critical path, not the core count, bounds build time. Once enough cores exist, wall time is the longest dependency chain. Predicts: why scheduling priority should favor critical-path nodes. Bug without it: a naive FIFO scheduler runs cheap leaf nodes first and stalls on the critical path. Applied: §8 (scheduler), ADR-08, §4.3 (scheduler throughput).

MM7. Content addressing makes the cache deduplicating and verifiable for free. A blob is named by its hash, so identical content stored once and integrity is self-checking. Predicts: the ≤ 1.05× storage amplification. Bug without it: storing by path/name duplicates identical outputs and cannot detect corruption. Applied: §8 (CAS), §4.1 (CAS amplification), ADR-04.

MM8. A null build must be nearly free, or incrementality is a lie. With zero dirty nodes the engine should stat inputs and exit in milliseconds, never hash everything. Predicts: the ≤ 50 ms null-build target. Bug without it: re-fingerprinting the whole tree every invocation makes "incremental" builds O(repo size). Applied: §4.1 / §4.3 (null build), Phase 4, MM2.

MM9. Persisted state is a cache of the graph, reconstructable by re-evaluation. If the incremental DB is corrupt, full re-eval is always a correct fallback. Predicts: the ≤ 10 s crash-recovery path. Bug without it: trusting a corrupt persisted state poisons every subsequent build. Applied: §4.6 (corrupt DB → full re-eval), Phase 9, §4.1 (persist read/write).

MM10. Non-determinism is the cache's enemy; detect it before it poisons. An action that produces different outputs from identical inputs breaks MM1; re-exec-and-compare catches it. Predicts: the reproducibility-check design. Bug without it: a non-deterministic action caches one of its outputs and serves it forever, masking real changes. Applied: ADR-25 (repro check), §4.6 (non-deterministic), Phase 13–14.

Comparative Architecture Table

This design (content-addressed action graph + fingerprint early-cutoff + sandboxed exec + REAPI remote cache) versus four real build systems. Real systems only.

Dimension This blueprint (ibg) Bazel Buck2 Ninja Nx / Turborepo
Action model hermetic, content-addressed hermetic, content-addressed hermetic, content-addressed command + deps (non-hermetic) task graph + hashing
Incrementality fingerprint + early cutoff digest-based + cutoff digest-based + cutoff mtime + dep files input-hash task cache
Cycle handling detect + error detect + error detect + error detect + error detect + error
Sandboxing namespaces/seccomp/Seatbelt sandbox (linux-sandbox) sandbox none none
Remote cache REAPI v2 REAPI v2 (native) REAPI v2 none (local only) provider cloud cache
Scheduler Chase-Lev + critical-path dynamic, skyframe rust, async dynamic topological, simple task-level parallel
Scope general action graph general, polyglot general, polyglot low-level executor JS/TS monorepo tasks

Bazel is the canonical hermetic, content-addressed build system with Skyframe incremental evaluation and native REAPI; it wins for large polyglot monorepos demanding correctness and remote execution. This blueprint reimplements Bazel's core ideas (MM1–MM4) at teachable scale, deferring remote execution and Starlark (Deferred Features Register).

Buck2 (Meta) is a Rust rewrite with a fully dynamic, async dependency graph and strong remote-execution integration; it wins where dynamic dependencies and scale matter. We share Buck2's Rust + async lineage but keep a simpler static-then-incremental graph for clarity, deferring dynamic-dependency discovery.

Ninja is a minimal, fast executor fed by generated build files, using mtime + depfiles rather than content hashing; it wins as a low-level backend (CMake/Meson target) where the front-end already computed the graph. We instead own fingerprinting and early cutoff (MM2–MM3) for correctness Ninja deliberately trades away for speed and simplicity.

Nx / Turborepo specialize in JS/TS monorepo task caching with input-hash-based memoization and cloud caches; they win for front-end monorepos with task-level (not file-level) granularity. We operate at the general action/file granularity with sandboxing (MM4), trading JS-ecosystem ergonomics for language-agnostic hermeticity.

Back-of-Envelope Capacity Model

Numbers an expert computes before designing, each derived from §4.

  • C1 — Node arena memory. 1 M nodes × ~200 B (§4.2) = ~200 MB; with adjacency (~160 MB) and NodeState (~64 MB) the graph core is ~424 MB of the 2 GB ceiling (§4.1).
  • C2 — Edge memory. 10 M edges × 16 B (from+to, §4.2) = 160 MB; NodeId(u32) caps the graph at 4 GiB of node-id space (§4.1), far above the 1 M-node target.
  • C3 — Null-build budget. ≤ 50 ms for 1 M nodes (§4.1) ⇒ ≤ 50 ns/node of stat-fast-path work — far too little to hash, which is why mtime is the hint and content hashing is skipped when clean (MM2/MM8).
  • C4 — Fingerprint throughput. ≥ 500 MB/s (§4.1, blake3 SIMD): re-fingerprinting a 1 GB working set on a cold cache takes ≈ 2 s, bounding the corrupt-DB recovery path (§4.6).
  • C5 — Scheduler dispatch rate. ≤ 500 µs/node dispatch (§4.1) and ≥ 50 k actions/s (§4.3) ⇒ a 100 k-action build spends ≈ 2 s purely in scheduling, exclusive of execution.
  • C6 — Cache hit economics. ≥ 95% warm hit (§4.3): for a 1 M-node clean build, ≥ 950 k nodes are fetched/skipped and ≤ 50 k executed — the difference between a 30 s cached build (§4.3) and hours of cold execution.
  • C7 — CAS storage amplification. ≤ 1.05× (§4.1): content addressing means identical outputs across nodes/builds are stored once; the 5% overhead is index + small-blob padding (MM7).
  • C8 — Persist write budget. ≤ 200 ms for 100 k dirty nodes (§4.1) ⇒ ≤ 2 µs/node in a batched transaction with async fsync — batching is mandatory (per-node fsync would be ~1000× slower).
  • C9 — Remote cache RTT vs local exec. RTT p99 ≤ 200 ms/lookup (§4.1): a remote lookup only pays off if it saves more than 200 ms of local execution, so cheap actions should skip remote and execute locally (§4.6 fallback).
  • C10 — File-descriptor budget. ≤ 1024 fds/build (§4.1); inotify watches capped at min(rlimit/2, 8192) (§4.4) — beyond which paths fall back to polling, trading latency for fd headroom.
  • C11 — Incremental 1%-dirty cost. 10 k of 1 M nodes dirty ⇒ ≤ 500 ms eval+schedule excl. exec (§4.3): early cutoff (MM3) keeps re-evaluation proportional to the changed subgraph, not the whole graph.
  • C12 — Daemon heap ceiling. ≤ 2 GB total (§4.2) with jemalloc (ADR-30) to bound fragmentation across long-running watch-loop builds; OOM is a hard ResourceExhausted error, not a swap death-spiral.

Hard Questions (Self-Examination)

Each requires synthesizing ≥ 2 sections.

  1. Why is the action key a pure function of inputs, and what stale-hit bug appears if an input is omitted? (MM1 + §8 + §4.3)
  2. Why is content fingerprinting required for correctness when mtime is faster? (MM2 + Phase 5 + §4.1)
  3. What is early cutoff, and why does a comment-only edit rebuild almost nothing? (MM3 + Phase 6 + §4.3)
  4. Why is hermeticity the precondition for a sound cache, and how is it enforced? (MM4 + Phase 8 + §4.6)
  5. Trace a 1%-dirty incremental build from file event to executed actions, naming each crate and budget. (§3 + §4.3 + Phase 4–6)
  6. Why does the critical path bound wall time once enough cores exist, and how does the scheduler exploit that? (MM6 + ADR-08 + §4.3)
  7. Why is content addressing both deduplicating and self-verifying? (MM7 + §4.1 CAS + ADR-04)
  8. Derive the null-build per-node time budget and explain why hashing everything is impossible there. (MM8 + Capacity C3 + §4.1)
  9. What makes a corrupt persisted DB always recoverable, and at what cost? (MM9 + §4.6 + Capacity C4)
  10. How is a non-deterministic action detected before it poisons the cache? (MM10 + ADR-25 + §4.6)
  11. Why does blake3 vs SHA-256 matter, and when would you still choose SHA-256? (ADR-04 + Comparative (REAPI) + §4.1 fingerprint)
  12. How do local and remote caches interact, and when is a remote lookup not worth it? (Capacity C9 + §4.6 + ADR-12)
  13. What bounds the graph at 1 M nodes / 10 M edges, and what is the memory cost? (Capacity C1/C2 + §4.1 + §4.2)
  14. Why is the scheduler custom rather than Rayon? (MM6 + ADR-08 + §4.4)
  15. How does this design differ from Ninja's, and what correctness does it buy? (Comparative Architecture + MM2/MM4 + Alternative Architectures)
  16. Why must persist writes be batched, quantitatively? (Capacity C8 + §4.1 + Phase 9)
  17. What happens on an undeclared-input detection, and why fail loudly? (MM4 + §4.6 + Phase 8)
  18. How is the ≥ 95% cache hit rate the economic difference between a 30 s and an hours-long build? (Capacity C6 + §4.3 + MM1)
  19. Why is remote execution deferred while remote caching is in scope? (Deferred Features Register + ADR-12 + §1 scope)
  20. How does the watch-loop daemon stay within the 2 GB heap over a long session? (Capacity C12 + ADR-30 + §4.2)

Alternative Architectures (Roads Not Taken)

Alt-A — Timestamp-based incremental (Make/Ninja model).

target rebuilt iff any prerequisite mtime > target mtime; depfiles track discovered headers

Wins when: the build is local, single-machine, and speed/simplicity beat cross-machine cache correctness (a low-level executor behind CMake). Embodied by: Make, Ninja. Rejected because: mtime is unsound across machines and clock skew (MM2) and cannot support a shared content-addressed cache (MM1, MM7); content hashing is the price of correctness and remote caching.

Alt-B — Functional/demand-driven build (Shake/Nix model).

build = pure function; outputs demanded lazily; dependencies discovered dynamically during evaluation

Wins when: dependencies are dynamic/data-dependent and a purely functional, monadic build description fits (Haskell ecosystems, Nix derivations). Embodied by: Shake, Nix. Rejected (partly deferred) because: a static-then-incremental DAG (MM5) is simpler to schedule and reason about at this scope; dynamic dependency discovery is a deferred extension.

Alt-C — Remote execution fleet (not just caching).

actions dispatched to a worker farm; inputs uploaded to CAS; outputs downloaded; local machine orchestrates

Wins when: the build is too large for one machine and a worker fleet amortizes compute (huge monorepos). Embodied by: Bazel RBE, BuildBuddy, EngFlow. Rejected (deferred) because: remote caching gives most of the benefit at a fraction of the complexity; the worker fleet is in the Deferred Features Register.

Alt-D — Language-integrated incremental (compiler-internal, Salsa/rustc).

incremental computation inside one process: query system memoizes derived values with red-green tracking

Wins when: incrementality is needed within a single compiler/IDE process at sub-file granularity (rust-analyzer, rustc). Embodied by: Salsa, rustc query system. Rejected because: this is a cross-process build orchestrator at the action/file granularity; in-process query incrementality solves a different (finer) problem, though it shares the early-cutoff idea (MM3).

Deferred Features Register

Every PhaseDeferred("Deferred Features Register") anchor resolves here. Each row is out of the Phase-0–14 scope.

Feature Anchor Building Phase (if undeferred) Un-defer Trigger
Remote execution worker fleet this Register row New ibg-remote-exec crate (REAPI execution) Builds outgrow a single machine and a worker farm is justified (Alt-C)
Starlark/BUILD-file parser integration this Register row New ibg-starlark front-end Users author builds in Starlark rather than the native graph API
Distributed graph partitioning Scalability Analysis Graph sharding layer A single process cannot hold a graph beyond the 1 M-node ceiling
Query language (rdeps, allpaths, filter) this Register row ibg query engine extension Users need Bazel-style graph queries for analysis
Windows sandbox (job objects + NTFS filters) this Register row Windows sandbox backend (cf. ADR-10/ADR-11) First-class Windows hermetic execution is required
Persistent daemon with gRPC API this Register row ibg-daemon crate IDE/CI integrations need a long-running daemon endpoint

Glossary

Term Definition
Action Hermetic unit of work: (command, declared inputs, declared outputs, env, tool digest) → produces output files. Abstract; language-agnostic.
ActionKey Digest = blake3(command ‖ sorted_input_digests ‖ env_hash ‖ tool_digest). Content-addressed cache key for an action invocation. Identical ActionKey → identical outputs (hermeticity requirement).
Action Cache ActionKey → CacheEntry map (local + remote). Hit: materialize outputs from CAS, skip exec. Miss: execute and populate.
Build Graph DAG of BuildNodes: sources, actions, globs, groups. Edges encode declared dependency relationships. Acyclicity enforced at construction and at dynamic-dep insertion.
CAS (Content-Addressed Store) Blob store keyed by Digest. CAS object retrieved only by hash; no mutable keys. Shared between local cache and remote cache. Supports both local-disk and remote (REAPI) backends.
CasObject { digest: Digest, bytes: Vec<u8> }. Atomic blob in the CAS. Integrity: stored hash must equal blake3(bytes).
Clean (NodeState) last_verified == current_revision AND all transitive inputs verified. No rebuild needed. Corresponds to Salsa "green".
Critical Path Longest weighted path (by estimated or measured exec duration) through the ready DAG. ibg-sched prioritizes tasks on it to minimize wall-clock build time.
Cycle Freedom Invariant: the build graph is always a DAG. Dynamic dependency insertion that would form a cycle → IbgError::CycleDetected; never deadlocks scheduler.
Declared Inputs Set of NodeIds an action explicitly declares as inputs at graph-construction time. If an action reads a file NOT in declared inputs → undeclared-input violation, detected by sandbox, build fails loudly.
Declared Outputs Set of PathBufs an action promises to produce. Enforced post-exec: missing → IbgError::MissingOutput; extra files → undeclared-output warning or error (sandbox policy).
Demand-Driven / Pull-Based Eval Engine evaluates only nodes required by the build request (targets), pulling dependencies lazily. Unreachable nodes are never evaluated.
Dirty (NodeState) Node requires re-evaluation: either fingerprint changed, revision counter advanced, or transitive dirty ancestor found. Corresponds to Salsa "red".
Digest [u8; 32] — blake3 hash of file contents or serialized structures. Canonical fingerprint for change detection and cache keying.
Dynamic Dependency Edge { kind: Dynamic } discovered at action evaluation time (e.g., depfile or runtime-declared dep). Inserted into graph with discovered_at: Revision. Creates new incremental edges for future builds. Must not form cycles.
Early Cutoff When a dirty node is re-evaluated and its output Digest is unchanged vs. prior run, dependents remain Clean at current Revision. Propagation stops. Analogous to Shake's "changed" check and Salsa's "verified" shortcut.
Edge `{ from: NodeId, to: NodeId, kind: Static
Fan-in / Fan-out Fan-in: a node with many dependents (e.g., a widely-shared library). Fan-out: a node with many dependencies. High fan-in amplifies early-cutoff value; high fan-out amplifies parallelism.
Fingerprint Content digest of a file or structured value used to detect changes without reading full content where possible (stat fast-path: mtime+size shortcut before blake3).
Glob Node NodeKind::Glob { pattern }. Represents a file-set matched by a glob pattern. Invalidated when matched file set or contents change. Produces a list of source NodeIds.
Group Node NodeKind::Group. Aggregates multiple nodes under one logical target. No exec; just a structural alias for a set of deps.
Hermetic Action Action whose outputs depend only on declared inputs, command, env, and tools — no hidden filesystem reads, no network, no timestamps. Same ActionKey → byte-identical outputs.
ibg-core Root crate: fundamental types shared across all crates (NodeId, Revision, Digest, ActionKey, NodeState, IbgError, …). No logic, no I/O.
Incremental Computation Only the minimal subgraph affected by a change is re-evaluated. Based on revision-counter dirtiness propagation, memoized results, and early cutoff.
Invalidation Marking a node Dirty because its fingerprint changed or a dependency's last_changed advanced. Propagated forward through reverse-dep edges.
IbgError Top-level error enum covering: CycleDetected, UndeclaredInput, MissingOutput, ExecFailed, CacheCorrupt, PersistCorrupt, RemoteUnavailable, ResourceExhausted, Timeout, SchemaVersionMismatch.
Memoization Per-node storage of (ActionKey, output Digest, Revision). On next eval: if ActionKey matches stored key, return memoized result without exec. Cache lookup before memoization check.
Merkle Input Tree Hierarchical content-addressed tree of input file digests, computed by ibg-remote. Used for remote cache keying and remote execution input upload per REAPI spec.
Minimal Rebuild Property: a node re-executes IFF its ActionKey changed AND no cache hit exists. No spurious rebuilds; no missed rebuilds.
NodeId u32 — dense integer ID for a BuildNode. Stable within a persisted graph; remapped if the graph is rebuilt from scratch.
NodeKey Stable human-meaningful identity string for a node (e.g., "//src/lib:compile"). Maps to NodeId via the graph index. Used for persistent state correlation across invocations.
NodeKind enum { Source { path: PathBuf }, Action(ActionId), Glob { pattern: String }, Group }. Determines eval semantics.
NodeState `{ last_verified: Revision, last_changed: Revision, output_digest: Option<Digest>, status: Clean
Over-build Correctness bug: rebuilding a node whose inputs did not actually change. Wastes time; never produces wrong output. Prevented by fingerprint checks + memoization.
Ready Queue ibg-sched structure: set of actions whose declared inputs are all Clean. Actions dequeued by worker threads. Ordered by critical-path weight.
Red/Green (Revision Counters) Salsa-derived metaphor. last_changed < last_verified → green (Clean). last_changed == current_revision → red (Dirty). Revision monotonically increases each build cycle.
Remote Cache REAPI-compatible content-addressed cache reachable over gRPC. ibg-remote uploads (ActionKey, output tree) and queries before local exec. Hit: materialize locally from CAS.
Reproducibility Check Re-executing a hermetic action with same ActionKey and asserting output digests match prior run. Detects non-deterministic actions. Controlled by --check-determinism policy.
Revision u64 — monotonically increasing global counter. Incremented once per build invocation (or more finely per change detection cycle). Drives red/green accounting.
Reverse-Dependency Edge Edge to → from (inverted DAG). Used by ibg-watch and ibg-engine to propagate Dirty forward from a changed source to all transitive dependents.
Running (NodeState) Transient: action dispatched to ibg-exec, not yet complete. Prevents duplicate scheduling.
Salsa Red/green revision-based incremental query engine (rust-analyzer). Conceptual model for ibg-engine's last_verified/last_changed revision accounting and early cutoff.
Sandbox Execution environment enforcing hermeticity: intercepts syscalls or uses OS-level isolation (namespaces/seccomp on Linux, sandbox-exec on macOS) to detect undeclared reads/writes.
Source Node NodeKind::Source { path }. Leaf node: no dependencies. Digest computed by ibg-fingerprint from file contents. Invalidated by ibg-watch on fs event.
Stat Fast-Path Before computing blake3 of a file, check (mtime, size, inode). If unchanged vs. stored stat, reuse stored Digest without hashing. Reduces I/O on large unchanged trees.
Topo Sort Kahn's algorithm over the build DAG; used by ibg-graph for ordering and by ibg-sched for scheduling. Reverse-topo used for invalidation propagation.
Under-build Correctness bug: serving stale output without detecting that an input changed. Never acceptable. Prevented by exhaustive fingerprint checks + sandbox undeclared-input detection.
Undeclared Input A file read by an action at exec time but NOT listed in declared inputs. Breaks hermeticity; makes ActionKey non-deterministic. Detected by sandbox; build fails loud, never silently wrong.
Verified (NodeState) Synonym for Clean. last_verified advanced to current_revision after confirming no fingerprint change — even without re-execution (early-cutoff case).
Work-Stealing Scheduler ibg-sched model: each worker thread has a local deque of ready tasks; idle threads steal from the back of other deques. Minimizes contention; maximizes CPU utilization on irregular DAGs.

File Purpose
Cargo.toml Deps: ibg-core, platform-specific (seccomp/landlock on Linux; sandbox-exec on macOS).
src/lib.rs Sandbox, `SandboxConfig { mode: Strict
src/linux.rs Linux: landlock + seccomp filter; mount namespace; /proc restriction.
src/macos.rs macOS: sandbox-exec profile generation; deny file-read outside declared inputs.
src/undeclared_detector.rs Parse sandbox audit log / strace output; diff against declared inputs → report violations.
src/output_enforcer.rs Post-exec: assert declared outputs exist; assert no extra files in output dir.
src/policy.rs SandboxPolicy: allowlist of system paths (libc, /usr/lib); configurable.
tests/undeclared_input.rs Action reads file not in declared inputs → SandboxError::UndeclaredInput.
tests/missing_declared_output.rs Action omits declared output → SandboxError::MissingOutput.

ibg-remote/

File Purpose
Cargo.toml Deps: ibg-core, ibg-cache, tonic (gRPC), prost, tokio.
src/lib.rs RemoteClient, RemoteConfig { endpoint, tls, timeout }, RemoteError.
src/merkle.rs Merkle input tree construction: hash_directory(inputs) -> RootDigest.
src/upload.rs Upload missing CAS blobs to remote; batched via BatchUpdateBlobs.
src/download.rs Download CAS blobs from remote; verify digests on receipt.
src/action_cache_remote.rs REAPI GetActionResult / UpdateActionResult; map to CacheEntry.
src/remote_exec.rs REAPI Execute RPC; poll Operation stream; return ExecResult.
src/retry.rs Exponential backoff with jitter; circuit-breaker on repeated remote failure.
tests/mock_remote.rs In-process mock REAPI server; assert round-trip cache hit.
tests/merkle_correctness.rs Known directory tree → assert expected Merkle root digest.

ibg-telemetry/

File Purpose
Cargo.toml Deps: ibg-core, tracing, tracing-subscriber, opentelemetry.
src/lib.rs TelemetrySink, BuildMetrics, SpanRecorder.
src/metrics.rs Counters: cache_hits, cache_misses, actions_executed, nodes_cutoff_early.
src/critical_path_reporter.rs Compute and emit critical-path trace after build completes.
src/trace.rs tracing spans for eval, cache lookup, exec, sched dispatch.
src/profile.rs Chrome trace format export (trace.json); Gantt timeline of action spans.
src/events.rs BuildEvent enum emitted to subscribers (CLI, JSON logger, OpenTelemetry).
tests/metrics_collection.rs Run mini-build; assert cache_hits + cache_misses == actions_evaluated.

ibg-cli/

File Purpose
Cargo.toml Deps: all ibg-* crates; clap (arg parsing); tokio runtime.
src/main.rs Entry point; parse args; initialize engine, scheduler, watcher; run build loop.
src/args.rs clap-derived Args struct: --targets, --jobs, --cache-dir, --remote, --sandbox, --watch, --profile.
src/config.rs Merge CLI args + config file into EngineConfig, SchedConfig, CacheConfig.
src/watch_loop.rs Daemon mode: receive watch events → incremental rebuild → print results.
src/output.rs Human-readable build result printer; JSON output mode.
src/error_display.rs IbgError → user-facing message with actionable hints.
tests/cli_integration.rs End-to-end: build a tiny declared-action graph; assert outputs produced.

ibg-bench/

File Purpose
Cargo.toml Deps: all ibg-* crates; criterion, tempfile.
benches/large_graph.rs 10k-node synthetic DAG; measure full cold-build throughput.
benches/incremental_edit.rs Leaf edit in 10k-node graph; measure hot-build latency (nodes re-evaluated).
benches/cache_hit_rate.rs Repeated builds with no changes; assert 100% cache-hit rate; measure overhead.
benches/fingerprint_throughput.rs Hash 10k files of varying size; stat-cache hit/miss ratio.
benches/differential.rs Compare ibg-engine vs. naïve rebuild-all baseline; correctness + speedup factor.
src/generators.rs Synthetic graph generators: chain, diamond, wide-fan, random-sparse.
src/fixtures.rs Temp-dir fixtures with declared source files for exec-path benchmarks.

Root workspace

File Purpose
Cargo.toml Workspace manifest; [workspace.dependencies] pins shared crate versions.
Cargo.lock Locked dependency tree; committed to repo for reproducible CI builds.
rust-toolchain.toml Pins stable channel at MSRV 1.78; ensures uniform compiler across contributors.
.cargo/config.toml [build] rustflags; target-specific linker overrides; [profile.bench] opts.
FINAL_BLUEPRINT.md This specification document.
final_ranking.md Portfolio project ledger (external to ibg source).

Appendix B — Milestone Summary

Phase Deliverable Duration Key Risks Exit Criteria
0 Workspace scaffold; ibg-core types; all crate stubs compile 1 w Type design churn; IbgError variant completeness cargo build --workspace passes; all core types serialize round-trip
1 ibg-graph: DAG construction, cycle detection, topo sort, reverse-dep index 1.5 w Petgraph API impedance; dynamic-dep cycle edge cases Cycle detection tests pass; topo order verified on 1k-node graph
2 ibg-fingerprint: blake3 hashing, stat fast-path, DigestCache, ActionKey 1 w Stat-cache invalidation correctness on mtime granularity; cross-platform stat fields Known-vector tests pass; stat-cache hit rate ≥ 90% on unchanged tree
3 ibg-persist: WAL writer/reader, CRC crash-consistency, schema versioning 1.5 w fsync semantics on macOS; torn-write simulation in tests Crash-consistency test passes; schema-mismatch triggers clean rebuild
4 ibg-cache: local ActionCache + CasStore, integrity checks, GC/eviction 1.5 w sled/rocksdb API choice lock-in; eviction correctness Round-trip cache tests; eviction under budget limit; integrity rejection of corrupt blob
5 ibg-engine: demand-driven eval, memoization, dirty/verified, early cutoff 2 w Recursive eval stack overflow on deep graphs; async coordination with scheduler Early-cutoff diamond test passes; no node rebuilt when inputs unchanged
6 ibg-exec: action spawning, output capture, env sanitization, working dir 1 w Process leak on timeout; output-dir cleanup on failure Lifecycle test passes; missing-output error surfaced correctly
7 ibg-sched: work-stealing scheduler, critical-path ordering, backpressure 2 w Deadlock on dependency cycles (must be caught in ibg-graph); starvation on high fan-in Parallel-exec test: independent nodes overlap; dependency-ordering invariant holds
8 ibg-sandbox: platform sandboxing, undeclared-input detection, output enforcement 2 w macOS sandbox-exec profile brittleness; Linux landlock MSRV compat Undeclared-input test fails build loudly; missing-output detected post-exec
9 ibg-watch: fs-event integration, debounce, invalidation propagation, daemon 1.5 w inotify fd limits; FUSE polling fallback correctness Transitive dirty propagation test; rapid-write debounce coalesces to single event
10 ibg-remote: Merkle tree, REAPI upload/download, remote cache + exec, retry 2 w gRPC TLS setup in CI; mock-server fidelity vs. real Bazel RBE Mock-remote round-trip test; Merkle root determinism; circuit-breaker triggers on repeated failure
11 ibg-telemetry: metrics, tracing spans, critical-path report, Chrome trace export 1 w Span overhead on hot eval path; metric accuracy under parallelism cache_hits + cache_misses == actions_evaluated; trace JSON parseable by Perfetto
12 ibg-cli: arg parsing, config merge, watch-loop daemon, human + JSON output 1 w Config-precedence ambiguity; watch-loop graceful shutdown End-to-end CLI integration test; --watch daemon rebuilds on file edit
13 ibg-bench: large-graph throughput, incremental-edit latency, cache-hit-rate, differential 1.5 w Benchmark variance on CI hardware; synthetic graph unrepresentativeness Cold-build 10k-node ≤ 60s; hot-build leaf-edit ≤ 500ms; 100% cache-hit on repeat; speedup vs. naive ≥ 5×
